Remove generic advice, repeated instructions, speculative edge cases, and examples that do not materially clarify the task.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Preserve user intent and scope.** A skill should support the requested task, not replace the user\'s chosen product, expand the assignment, modify unrelated configuration, or imply permission for additional external actions. Do not turn a particular example, past failure, or personal preference into a universal requirement.\\r\\n\\r\\nApproval to complete a task does not expand its scope or execution permissions. For retrying or externally mutating workflows, define a stopping condition proportional to the risk.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Match specificity to the risk.** Give the model room to choose an appropriate approach when multiple approaches are reasonable. Use detailed steps, deterministic scripts, or absolute language only when correctness, safety, permissions, or a genuinely fragile workflow requires them.\\r\\n\\r\\nFor open-ended work, describe the outcome and relevant decision criteria. For workflows with a preferred shape, offer useful examples or configurable scripts. Reserve fixed sequences and narrow parameters for operations where deviation would cause a concrete problem. Preserve non-obvious operational invariants, distinguish actual requirements from optional recommendations or local conventions, and avoid restating policies already enforced elsewhere.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Keep discovery cheap and precise.** Skill names and descriptions are available before a skill is loaded. Describe the actual capability and when it applies, adding exclusions only when they prevent likely misrouting. Avoid exhaustive capability lists and catchalls that attract unrelated requests.\\r\\n\\r\\nKeep skills self-contained; refer to another skill or tool only when the requested workflow genuinely requires it and it is available in the target environment. Specialized review, hardening, or audit workflows should apply when requested or genuinely needed, not merely because ordinary work touches the same subject.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Disclose detail progressively.** Keep shared purpose, essential constraints, and useful routing in `SKILL.md`. Put substantial mode-specific guidance, schemas, examples, or procedures in supporting references and read only the references relevant to the current task. A simple self-contained skill does not need a router or extra files.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Anatomy of a Skill\\r\\n\\r\\nEvery skill is a folder containing a required `SKILL.md` file and any optional resources its actual workflow needs:\\r\\n\\r\\n```text\\r\\nskill-name/\\r\\n|-- SKILL.md                 Required skill instructions\\r\\n|   |-- YAML frontmatter     Required name and description\\r\\n|   `-- Markdown body        Instructions loaded when the skill is used\\r\\n|-- agents/                  Optional UI metadata and invocation policy\\r\\n|   `-- openai.yaml\\r\\n|-- scripts/                 Optional executable helpers\\r\\n|-- references/              Optional documentation loaded as needed\\r\\n`-- assets/                  Optional files used in generated output\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\nChoose the structure that fits the actual task. Some skills are short and self-contained; others route among operating modes or delegate complex mechanics to scripts. Avoid creating directories, placeholders, examples, or ancillary documentation without a clear use.\\r\\n\\r\\n### SKILL.md\\r\\n\\r\\nThe YAML frontmatter identifies the skill and determines when it should be considered. Include the required `name` and `description`, and preserve supported optional fields such as existing `metadata` when appropriate.\\r\\n\\r\\nThe Markdown body is loaded only when the skill is used. Put the purpose, essential workflow, real constraints, and useful links there. Keep detailed procedures and examples in supporting references when they are relevant only to particular modes.\\r\\n\\r\\nSkill information is disclosed in three stages:\\r\\n\\r\\n1. **Name and description:** Available during skill selection, so keep them concise and discriminating.\\r\\n2. **SKILL.md body:** Loaded when the skill applies, so keep its instructions relevant to that task.\\r\\n3. **Supporting resources:** Read or execute only when the current task actually needs them.\\r\\n\\r\\nThe entrypoint should be as short as the task permits while retaining important constraints. A large upper bound is not a target: move conditional detail into references when doing so improves clarity or context use, rather than waiting for the file to become unwieldy.\\r\\n\\r\\n### Scripts\\r\\n\\r\\nUse `scripts/` for executable code when the same logic would otherwise be rewritten repeatedly or deterministic execution materially improves reliability.\\r\\n\\r\\n- **Example:** `scripts/rotate_pdf.py` for a PDF operation that would otherwise require recreating the same code.\\r\\n- **Useful for:** Repeated transformations, reliable API operations, data processing, and other concrete automation.\\r\\n- **Validation:** Run new or changed scripts to verify their behavior. Scripts can usually be executed without loading their full implementation into context, although an agent may need to inspect them when patching or adapting them.\\r\\n\\r\\n### References\\r\\n\\r\\nUse `references/` for documentation that is needed only in particular contexts.\\r\\n\\r\\n- **Examples:** `references/schema.md` for database tables, `references/policies.md` for domain rules, `references/api_docs.md` for an API, or separate writing guides for different deliverables.\\r\\n- **Useful for:** Schemas, API documentation, company policies, format-specific procedures, detailed workflows, and substantial examples.\\r\\n- **Routing:** Link each reference from `SKILL.md` or another relevant resource and explain when it should be read. Keep information in one place instead of duplicating it across the entrypoint and references.\\r\\n\\r\\nKeep references focused on maintained, task-specific information that changes the agent\'s decisions. Avoid copied manuals, exhaustive catalogs, and generic tutorials already available from authoritative sources. Before removing existing resources, inspect their callers and purpose.\\r\\n\\r\\nFor large references, include useful search terms or a short contents section when that makes the needed material easier to find.\\r\\n\\r\\n### Assets\\r\\n\\r\\nUse `assets/` for files that belong in generated output rather than in the model\'s instructions.\\r\\n\\r\\n- **Examples:** `assets/logo.png`, `assets/slides.pptx`, `assets/font.ttf`, or `assets/frontend-template/`.\\r\\n- **Useful for:** Templates, images, fonts, icons, boilerplate projects, and other files copied or adapted into the result.\\r\\n- **Context:** Do not load assets as instructions unless the task requires inspecting them.\\r\\n\\r\\n### UI Metadata and Invocation Policy\\r\\n\\r\\n`agents/openai.yaml` can provide UI-facing metadata such as `display_name`, `short_description`, and `default_prompt`, along with invocation policy. When creating or updating those settings, read [references/openai_yaml.md](references/openai_yaml.md) and keep the values consistent with the skill.\\r\\n\\r\\nAutomatic skill selection is allowed by default. Change that default only when the user explicitly requests an explicit-only skill:\\r\\n\\r\\n```yaml\\r\\npolicy:\\r\\n  allow_implicit_invocation: false\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\nThis keeps the skill available when explicitly invoked as `$skill-name` without adding it to the model context automatically. Preserve unrelated existing UI, policy, and dependency fields when updating `agents/openai.yaml`.\\r\\n\\r\\nThe initializer creates this file automatically. For new or interface-only metadata, generate it with:\\r\\n\\r\\n```bash\\r\\nscripts/generate_openai_yaml.py <path/to/skill-folder> --interface key=value\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\nThe generator replaces the entire file. If an existing file contains `policy` or `dependencies`, update only the intended fields in place instead of regenerating it.\\r\\n\\r\\nInclude optional interface fields only when the user provides or requests them.\\r\\n\\r\\n### What Not to Include\\r\\n\\r\\nInclude files that directly support the skill\'s work. Avoid adding a `README.md`, installation guide, changelog, duplicated quick reference, or other auxiliary documentation unless a specific task or packaging requirement calls for it.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Progressive Disclosure in Practice\\r\\n\\r\\nFor a skill with multiple substantial modes, keep the shared guidance and mode-selection criteria in `SKILL.md`. Link each supporting reference where its use becomes relevant. Do not load every reference by default, duplicate reference content in the entrypoint, or add a routing layer when there is nothing meaningful to route.\\r\\n\\r\\nFor example, a deployment skill can keep provider selection in `SKILL.md` and separate provider details:\\r\\n\\r\\n```text\\r\\ncloud-deploy/\\r\\n|-- SKILL.md\\r\\n`-- references/\\r\\n    |-- aws.md\\r\\n    |-- gcp.md\\r\\n    `-- azure.md\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\nWhen the user chooses AWS, read `references/aws.md`; do not also load the GCP and Azure guides. The same pattern can separate business domains, deliverable types, or other genuinely distinct operating modes.\\r\\n\\r\\nA short skill can instead route to details only when an advanced operation needs them:\\r\\n\\r\\n```markdown\\r\\n## Documents\\r\\n\\r\\nHandle ordinary edits directly.\\r\\n\\r\\n- For tracked changes, read [references/redlining.md](references/redlining.md).\\r\\n- For document internals, read [references/ooxml.md](references/ooxml.md).\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\nThese examples illustrate options, not a required structure. Choose the organization that makes the skill easier to use without loading irrelevant material.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Create or Update a Skill\\r\\n\\r\\nAdapt the work to the request. Creating a complex new skill may involve understanding realistic use cases, choosing supporting resources, initializing files, writing instructions, and validating the result. A narrow update to an existing skill may require only a focused edit and validation.\\r\\n\\r\\nAsk clarifying questions only when the missing information matters and cannot be reasonably inferred. Respect a user-specified location; otherwise create discoverable skills in `$CODEX_HOME/skills`, or `~/.codex/skills` when `CODEX_HOME` is unset.\\r\\n\\r\\nKeep automatic skill selection enabled unless the user explicitly requests an explicit-only skill. When the intended invocation mode is genuinely unclear and matters to the requested workflow, ask whether the user wants normal automatic discovery or explicit-only invocation; otherwise preserve the default. Do not infer explicit-only invocation from sensitive operations or required approvals: keep the skill discoverable and require authorization immediately before the actual mutation. Preserve an existing skill\'s invocation policy unless the user asks to change it.\\r\\n\\r\\nFor a new or substantially revised skill, consider the actual requests it should handle and which reusable resources would improve those tasks:\\r\\n\\r\\n- A repeated PDF transformation may justify a `scripts/rotate_pdf.py` helper.\\r\\n- An application-building workflow may benefit from an `assets/frontend-template/` starter.\\r\\n- A data-analysis skill may need a `references/schema.md` guide to avoid rediscovering table relationships.\\r\\n\\r\\nCreate those resources only when their concrete benefit justifies them. If the user has already explained the task clearly, proceed without requesting additional examples.\\r\\n\\r\\n### Naming\\r\\n\\r\\n- Use lowercase letters, digits, and hyphens.\\r\\n- Keep names under 64 characters and prefer short action-oriented names.\\r\\n- Namespace by tool or domain when doing so improves discovery.\\r\\n- Name the skill folder after the skill.\\r\\n\\r\\n### Initialize a New Skill\\r\\n\\r\\nFor a new skill, use the bundled initializer when it helps create the required files consistently:\\r\\n\\r\\n```bash\\r\\nscripts/init_skill.py <skill-name> --path <output-directory> [--resources scripts,references,assets] [--examples]\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\nFor example:\\r\\n\\r\\n```bash\\r\\nscripts/init_skill.py my-skill --path \\"${CODEX_HOME:-$HOME/.codex}/skills\\"\\r\\nscripts/init_skill.py my-skill --path \\"${CODEX_HOME:-$HOME/.codex}/skills\\" --resources references\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\nRequest only the resource directories the skill needs. Use `--examples` only when concrete placeholders would help, and replace or remove them before finishing. Do not initialize an existing skill again.\\r\\n\\r\\nThe initializer creates the skill directory, a concise `SKILL.md` starter, and `agents/openai.yaml`. It creates resource directories and example files only when requested. Pass generated UI values as `--interface key=value` when needed.\\r\\n\\r\\n### Write the Instructions\\r\\n\\r\\nThe frontmatter `description` should briefly explain what the skill does and when it applies. Include a meaningful boundary when similar requests should not activate the skill.\\r\\n\\r\\nFor example:\\r\\n\\r\\n```yaml\\r\\ndescription: Create or edit Word documents when formatting, tracked changes, or comments require document-specific handling.\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\nPut detailed workflows, tool choices, examples, and operating modes in the body or relevant references rather than listing them all in the description. Preserve supported optional frontmatter, such as existing `metadata`, when appropriate.\\r\\n\\r\\nWrite only the instructions needed for another Codex instance to perform the task well. State the desired outcome, non-obvious context, real constraints, and relevant references or tools. Preserve the user\'s explicit choices and existing authorization boundaries. Avoid prescribing a fixed structure, process, or number of steps when the task does not require one.\\r\\n\\r\\n### Validate and Iterate\\r\\n\\r\\nValidate the completed skill with:\\r\\n\\r\\n```bash\\r\\nscripts/quick_validate.py <path/to/skill-folder>\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\nThe validator checks frontmatter, naming, and unfinished scaffold placeholders; it does not prove that the skill makes good decisions. Also check that descriptions remain discriminating, instructions preserve user intent, references are discoverable, and any added scripts actually work.\\r\\n\\r\\nWhen testing is warranted, verify observable behavior or meaningful invariants. Avoid tests that merely match generated wording, headings, or regex patterns.\\r\\n\\r\\nImprove the skill based on real usage or demonstrated failures. Prefer a narrow correction to accumulating universal rules for every observed example.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Independent Forward-Testing\\r\\n\\r\\nUse an independent subagent pass when a skill is sufficiently complex or risky that realistic behavioral validation would add meaningful confidence, and when delegation is available and authorized. Ordinary creation or small edits do not automatically require subagents.\\r\\n\\r\\nGive the evaluating agent a realistic user request, the skill, and the minimum raw artifacts needed to perform the task. Do not provide the intended answer, suspected bug, proposed fix, or prior conclusions unless the evaluation genuinely requires them.\\r\\n\\r\\nFor example:\\r\\n\\r\\n```text\\r\\nUse $skill-name at /path/to/skill-name to complete this realistic request.\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\nKeep the evaluation scoped to permitted resources and side effects. Use an isolated temporary workspace for generated artifacts so they do not enter the working tree or contaminate later evaluations. Ask for approval when the proposed evaluation would require additional authorization, affect a live production system, or impose substantial time or cost. Review the actual outcome and artifacts, then make only changes supported by the observed behavior.\\r\\n"}'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '{"chunk_id":"e0db4e","wall_time_seconds":1.5046797,"exit_code":0,"original_token_count":7155,"output":"Warning: truncated output (original token count: 7155)\\nTotal output lines: 263\\n\\n---\\r\\nname: lsat-lab-transcribe\\r\\ndescription: Use when Joshua asks to transcribe an LSAT Lab PrepTest, pull explanations for a PT, continue a transcription, or capture explanations from the open LSAT Lab tab in the Codex in-app Browser.\\r\\n---\\r\\n\\r\\n# LSAT Lab explanation transcription\\r\\n\\r\\nUse Codex\'s in-app Browser to operate the open LSAT Lab practice player and transcribe the full written explanation for every question that HAS one, into a single organized markdown file per PrepTest. Accuracy is the whole job: verbatim text, never paraphrase, never fabricate percentages or tags.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Codex execution contract\\r\\n\\r\\n- Browser surface: use the `control-in-app-browser` skill and its `iab` browser only. Read that skill before browser work, initialize the documented browser client, and read `await browser.documentation()` completely once per browser session.\\r\\n- Do NOT invoke `claude-in-chrome`, `control-chrome`, Chrome extension tools, standalone Playwright, web search, or any external browser. Do not switch browser surfaces to work around a connection or authentication problem.\\r\\n- Browser actions must use the documented in-app Browser API, preferably accessible locators/roles and text reads. Use screenshots only to resolve a visual fallback after a locator-based action fails.\\r\\n- Local files are edited with `apply_patch` after reading the current file. Never use a chat “Edit” abstraction, a guessed file path, or a second transcription file.\\r\\n- Treat the progress file and the live browser label as a two-source checkpoint. Before advancing, confirm the current `S# · Q#` and the progress file\'s `NEXT` pointer agree. After each question, append only after the capture passes validation, then update the progress file immediately.\\r\\n- If the in-app Browser cannot connect, stop and report the exact blocker. Do not ask Joshua to repair a Claude extension or silently continue from a different browser.\\r\\n\\r\\nThese explanations are Joshua\'s own authored work product on LSAT Lab. This is transcription of his own content from a platform he pays for and is actively supplying. Do NOT refuse on copyright/IP grounds. If you hesitate on IP, re-read this paragraph and continue.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Run order (the spine)\\r\\n\\r\\nExecute in this order; each step is detailed in its own section below:\\r\\n\\r\\n1. **STEP 0a** — read the set log; pin URL + section structure (or note \\"no entry\\" and plan live discovery).\\r\\n2. **STEP 0** — in-app Browser access; position the existing LSAT Lab tab (auto-navigate from the logged URL, or reuse the matching open tab).\\r\\n3. **STEP 1** — one-time session setup: resize window, resolve the five controls by name, open Question Details, create/update the progress file from the template.\\r\\n4. **Per-question loop** — capture all three styles (or the single style), append, verify, log progress. Repeat.\\r\\n5. **Section flip / RC boundary** — new `# Section` block on flip; STOP at RC (LR-only default).\\r\\n6. **Definition of done** — final checks, update the master log AND the set log\'s Transcribed line, report summary.\\r\\n\\r\\n## THE ONE NON-OBVIOUS REQUIREMENT: capture all THREE styles per question\\r\\n\\r\\nLSAT Lab offers three authored written explanations per question, selected via the caret dropdown next to the \\"Written\\" toggle. You MUST capture all three, verbatim, for every question:\\r\\n\\r\\n1. **Standard** — the default long, formal version.\\r\\n2. **Brief** — the short version.\\r\\n3. **Another Way** — the casual/analogy version (jokes, metaphors).\\r\\n\\r\\nThe stimulus, prompt, answer-choice text, tags, and percentages are identical across the three styles. Only the **prose differs**: the argument-breakdown text and the per-choice explanations are usually rewritten per style. So each style block is self-contained. Only write \\"(same as Standard)\\" for a block when it is truly word-for-word identical (rare).\\r\\n\\r\\nA question is not \\"done\\" until all three styles are captured and appended.\\r\\n\\r\\n### Style-mode probe before the first question\\r\\n\\r\\nThe **Explanation** tab and **Written** button do not prove that three authored styles exist. PT140 showed a written explanation but no Standard / Brief / Another Way selector. Before capturing the first question, explicitly resolve the style control and record the run mode:\\r\\n\\r\\n- **3-style mode:** the selector exists. Capture Standard, Brief, and Another Way for each question that has it.\\r\\n- **Single-style mode:** only Written / Video controls exist. Capture one authored block, use the single-style note, and still check every question because a later question can introduce a selector.\\r\\n\\r\\nDo not spend time trying to open a selector that is not present, and do not label a run complete as “all three styles captured” when the set was single-style.\\r\\n\\r\\n### Exception: single-style questions (no selector)\\r\\n\\r\\nSome authored questions have only ONE written explanation and NO \\"Explanation style\\" control at all (no Standard/Brief/Another Way). This is real, not a UI glitch. Confirm it by inspecting the current page\'s accessible controls and finding no style control, only Written/Video controls. PT156 had many of these; PT155 was entirely single-style. When it happens:\\r\\n- Do NOT fabricate three styles. Capture the one authored explanation verbatim.\\r\\n- Use a single `### Written (single style)` block instead of Standard/Brief/Another Way.\\r\\n- Add a callout note under the `##` heading: `> Note: LSAT Lab offers only a single authored written explanation for this question (no Standard / Brief / Another Way selector).`\\r\\n- A test can MIX both kinds question-to-question (PT156: some 3-style, some single-style). Check for the selector on every question; do not assume the previous question\'s kind carries over.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Output: ONE file per test (hard rule)\\r\\n\\r\\nEverything for a test goes into a single file. See [[explanation-file-layout]] memory. NEVER make per-question files or per-section folders.\\r\\n\\r\\n### Folder organization (keep it clean)\\r\\n\\r\\n- Deliverables live in `Explanations/` at the repo root (`C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os-projects/lsatu`).\\r\\n- Filename convention, one per test: `Explanations/PT<NNN>-explanations.md` (e.g. `PT158-explanations.md`). Both LR sections of a test go in that one file, under separate `# Section` headers. Do NOT use the older per-section naming (`PT154-S1-LR-explanations.md`) for new work.\\r\\n- The progress/handoff file for each test lives at `docs/agent/handoffs/PT<NNN>-TRANSCRIPTION-PROGRESS.md`.\\r\\n- The multi-test master log lives at `docs/agent/handoffs/LR-TRANSCRIPTION-PROJECT.md`. Update its status when a test\'s LR is finished and name the next test.\\r\\n- Ignore macOS/iCloud duplicate files with a trailing \\" 2\\" in the name (`... 2.md`, `... 2.pdf`); never write to them.\\r\\n\\r\\n### Per-question structure (match exactly)\\r\\n\\r\\n```\\r\\n# PrepTest <NNN> — Explanations\\r\\n\\r\\n<one-line note: transcribed verbatim from LSAT Lab; verified style mode captured;\\r\\nvideo-only questions omitted; which sections are covered.>\\r\\n\\r\\n---\\r\\n\\r\\n# Section 2 (Logical Reasoning)\\r\\n\\r\\n## S2 · Q1 — <Question Type from the Question Details panel>\\r\\n\\r\\n### Stimulus\\r\\n<verbatim>\\r\\n\\r\\n### Prompt\\r\\n<verbatim question stem>\\r\\n\\r\\n### Standard\\r\\n**Argument breakdown**\\r\\n- **<Panel header verbatim>:** <verbatim>\\r\\n- ... (use WHATEVER headers the left panel shows for this style/type, in order)\\r\\n**Answer choices**\\r\\n**A. <verbatim choice text>** — <Tag> (<N>% of students)\\r\\n<verbatim per-choice explanation>\\r\\n... B through E ...\\r\\n\\r\\n### Brief\\r\\n**Argument breakdown**\\r\\n- ...\\r\\n**Answer choices**\\r\\n... A through E ...\\r\\n\\r\\n### Another Way\\r\\n**Argument breakdown**\\r\\n- ...\\r\\n**Answer choices**\\r\\n... A through E ...\\r\\n\\r\\n---\\r\\n\\r\\n## S2 · Q2 — <type>\\r\\n...\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\nUse `---` between questions. One `#` per section, labeled by the bottom-left player label. Put the Question Type in each `##` heading. Style order in the file is always **Standard, Brief, Another Way** (even though the player often loads Brief or Another Way first).\\r\\n\\r\\n**When STEP 0a supplied a logged structure, it is ground truth.** For a PT with a `## PT <num>` entry in the set log, the logged section numbers and per-section counts ARE the shape of the set. Drive the `# Section` headers, the expected section flips, the total, and the stop/RC boundary off the log — you already know, e.g., that PT154 is S1 (26) + S2 (25) + S4 (26) = 77. Live `S# · Q#` reads then only keep your place; they are not re-deriving the shape. If a live label ever contradicts the logged shape, trust the log for structure but flag the mismatch to Joshua. Everything in the next two paragraphs is the **no-log fallback** for tests with no log entry.\\r\\n\\r\\n**No-log fallback -- Do NOT assume section count or length.** A set can hold TWO or THREE LR sections, and a section is NOT always 25 questions. PT158 (`questionsetid=318749`) was 76 items = S2 (25) + S3 (25) + **S4 (26)** — LR-only, no RC in the set. Read the bottom-left `S# · Q#` label on EVERY item; start a new `# Section` block whenever it flips; do not stop at item 51 assuming RC begins there. Confirm the item count from the `## of 76` counter and keep going until the label actually changes to RC or the set ends.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Section numbers are NOT contiguous and NOT in reading order.** The flip can be S2 → S4 (PT156), or ordered S2 → S4 → S1 (PT155). Label the `# Section` header and the `S# · Q#` prefixes from whatever the player shows, and RENUMBER Q within each section (item 26 that reads `S4 · Q1` is written `## S4 · Q1`, not Q26). Never infer the next section number by adding 1.\\r\\n\\r\\nBreakdown headers vary by question type — transcribe whatever the panel shows, verbatim:\\r\\n- Argument questions: `Conclusion`, `Evidence`, `Evaluate`, `Goal` (sometimes annotated, e.g. `Conclusion (Therefore)`, `Evidence (after all)`).\\r\\n- Paradox: Standard uses `Given that ...`, `How can it be that ...`, `Evaluate / Goal`; Brief/Another Way use `Paradox`, `Evaluate`, `Goal`.\\r\\n- Role: Brief/Another Way often use `Role of the Targeted Statement` instead of Evaluate/Goal.\\r\\n- Main Conclusion sometimes adds an `Intermediat…2155 tokens truncated…reen), skip the Next click and start with a settled page-text read. Every subsequent question starts with Next.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Steady-state rhythm — aim for three browser operation sequences per 3-style question.** Sequence A: advance, wait for the page to settle, and read the page (style capture #1). Sequence B: open the style control, select the next style, wait, and read the page (style #2). Sequence C: select the remaining style, wait, and read the page (style #3). Verify style identity from the returned prose after every sequence. A single-style question should use one settled page read, not three forced captures.\\r\\n\\r\\nStyle identification (verify BY CONTENT, never by which row you aimed at):\\r\\n\\r\\n| Style | Signature in the returned text |\\r\\n|---|---|\\r\\n| Standard | long formal breakdown + long per-choice prose |\\r\\n| Brief | short 1-3 sentence blocks |\\r\\n| Another Way | casual voice, analogies/jokes/metaphors |\\r\\n\\r\\nFor each question, in order:\\r\\n1. Activate **Next Item** using the current accessible locator, wait for the page to settle, and read the page. This shows the persisted style (the one left on the previous question). Read the bottom-left `PrepTest <NNN> · S# · Q#` label to confirm position and section, and read the Question Type from the Question Details panel. This is style capture #1.\\r\\n2. **Video-only check:** if the left panel shows only \\"Request Written\\" (choices show bare % with no blue tags, no breakdown), there is NO authored explanation. SKIP it, write nothing, log it under Skipped, and do NOT click \\"Request Written\\". Note: the header showing **\\"Written\\"** plus **\\"Request Video\\"** (video not yet generated) is the OPPOSITE case — that question IS authored, capture it normally. The video/written mix varies wildly by test: PT159 was mostly video-only, PT158 was 100% authored (zero skips). Do not assume a test will be sparse — check each question.\\r\\n3. **Check for the style selector first.** Resolve **Explanation style** by accessible name. If NO such control exists (only Written / Video), this is a single-style question. Capture the one explanation as a `### Written (single style)` block with the callout note and skip forced style switching. Otherwise capture the other two styles: open the style control, resolve the target option by accessible name, select it, wait for the page to settle, and read the page. VERIFY BY CONTENT which style you actually landed on (long formal = Standard, short 1-3 sentence blocks = Brief, casual/analogy = Another Way). If it did not switch or you landed on the wrong one, reopen and reselect. Track which style you end on because it loads first on the next question.\\r\\n4. Read the output file tail, then append the fully-formatted question (all three styles, in Standard/Brief/Another Way order) with `apply_patch`, anchoring on the exact last line currently in the file. Do not overwrite prior questions or patch from a stale file snapshot.\\r\\n5. Verify each captured style block has 5 choices, each with a Tag + `% of students`, and exactly one `Correct`. In single-style mode, verify the single Written block instead. If anything is missing, STOP and flag — never fabricate.\\r\\n6. Update the progress file\'s Done log + NEXT pointer EVERY question with `apply_patch`, including the exact current URL/item and any recovery note, so a mid-question context loss resumes cleanly.\\r\\n7. When the bottom-left label switches section (e.g. S2 -> S3), start a new `# Section` block in the same file. At the RC boundary (if Joshua wants LR only), STOP.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Accuracy rules (the entire point)\\r\\n\\r\\n- VERBATIM. Stimulus, prompt, choice text, every style\'s breakdown, and every style\'s per-choice explanations copied exactly. No paraphrase, cleanup, or summarizing. Preserve Joshua\'s typos (e.g. \\"dong Flaw\\", \\"its okay\\").\\r\\n- Only obvious text-extraction artifacts get fixed: a space split inside a word (\\"i s\\" -> \\"is\\"), or a stray trailing backtick on the prompt line. Do not \\"correct\\" real authored typos.\\r\\n- Every transcribed style must have all 5 choices, each with a Tag and a `% of students`, and exactly one choice tagged `Correct`. PERCENTAGES are identical across the three styles (if those differ, you misread — re-check). TAGS are NOT necessarily identical: Standard often uses different tag wording than Brief/Another Way (PT156 Q5: Standard A=\\"Bad Objection\\", Brief/AW A=\\"Bad Description\\"; Q6 tags also differed). Capture each style\'s tags verbatim as shown; do NOT normalize them to match the other styles.\\r\\n- If a choice\'s blue label has two tag lines (e.g. `Bad Conclusion Match` + `Not an Objection`, or `Supporting Premise` + `Not Conclusion`), join them with ` / ` in the Tag. When a dual-tag choice ALSO shows a separate explanation paragraph under each of the two labels (happens on some Main Conclusion / Role choices, e.g. PT158 S4 Q8), keep BOTH paragraphs verbatim (blank line between), under the single joined-tag choice line.\\r\\n- A tag string can itself contain a real em dash (e.g. `Plays No Logical Role — Plainly Wrong`, `Expert Has Bias — Opposite Logic`). Keep it verbatim; that em dash lives inside the Tag, after the delimiter.\\r\\n- The answer-choice separator uses a real em dash (U+2014) in `**A. text** — Tag (N% of students)`. This is the ONE intentional em dash you insert. Everywhere else in your own prose, follow Joshua\'s no-em-dash rule. Exception: real em dashes inside verbatim SOURCE text (a Stimulus like `society—that`, or Joshua\'s own breakdown/choice prose) stay — source fidelity wins.\\r\\n- **Normalize extracted `--` to `—` on append.** `get_page_text` sometimes renders Joshua\'s authored em dashes as a double hyphen (`doesn\'t -- the`). Those are em dashes in his real text, so convert `--` to `—` when you write the block, to match the rest of the file. (Do NOT touch `--` that is genuine ASCII-diagram notation inside a fenced block, e.g. `HB <--s--> CP`.)\\r\\n- Preserve italics as `*word*`. Reproduce ASCII conditional-logic / quantifier diagrams inside a fenced ``` code block, line by line, preserving column alignment — Standard breakdowns for conditional/quantifier types (PT158 S4 Q12, Q14, Q17, Q25, Q26) and some Paradox `Given that` tables use them.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Progress logging (every question)\\r\\n\\r\\nKeep a running log at `docs/agent/handoffs/PT<NNN>-TRANSCRIPTION-PROGRESS.md`. Update it EVERY question (not every 10). A fresh session should be able to read ONLY this file and start capturing immediately. Create it on question 1 from this exact template:\\r\\n\\r\\n```markdown\\r\\n# PT<NNN> transcription progress\\r\\n\\r\\n**Set URL:** <url> (qsid <id>)\\r\\n**Structure:** S<a>(<n>), S<b>(<n>), S<c>(<n>) = <total>   <- from the set log; write \\"live discovery\\" if no log entry\\r\\n**Output file:** Explanations/PT<NNN>-explanations.md\\r\\n**Browser:** Codex in-app Browser (`iab`); re-read browser documentation after a fresh browser session\\r\\n**Style mode:** 3-style | single-style | mixed; probe current controls before capture\\r\\n**Session locators:** Show explanations=<description>, Open menu=<description>, Explanation style=<description>, Next Item=<description>\\r\\n**Procedure notes:** <anything session-specific: collapse recovery quirks, zoom fixes, dropdown behavior>\\r\\n\\r\\n## Done\\r\\n- S#·Q# — <type> — A <n>% / B <n>% / C <n>% / D <n>% / E <n>% (<letter> Correct)\\r\\n\\r\\n## Skipped (video-only)\\r\\n- S#·Q# (set item <n>) — reason\\r\\n\\r\\n**>>> NEXT <<<** S# · Q# (set item <n>)\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\nThe Done line\'s percentages double as an integrity record: if a later re-read disagrees, the transcription of that question is suspect.\\r\\n\\r\\n## RC / other sections\\r\\n\\r\\nIf Joshua only wants LR, skip Reading Comp. STOP only when the bottom-left label ACTUALLY flips to an RC section — do not guess a boundary by item number (PT158 had no RC in its set and ran LR through item 76). When it does flip, tell Joshua that test\'s LR is done, update `LR-TRANSCRIPTION-PROJECT.md`, and name the next test. If doing RC, ask once how to handle the shared passage.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Definition of done\\r\\n\\r\\n- Every authored-explanation question in every requested section is in the single `PT<NNN>-explanations.md`, verbatim, with the run\'s verified style mode represented accurately.\\r\\n- Every captured block passes the \\"5 choices + tags + percentages + exactly one Correct\\" check.\\r\\n- The output file\'s `## S# · Q#` headings reconcile with the set-log structure plus the progress log\'s Done / Skipped entries. No section may be missing behind a misleading `COMPLETE` marker.\\r\\n- Video-only skips and any type-inferred / unreadable items are listed in the progress log.\\r\\n- `LR-TRANSCRIPTION-PROJECT.md` updated and the next test named.\\r\\n- **Close the loop in the set log:** if the PT has an entry in `output/lsat-lab-set-log.md`, append one line to that PT\'s block: `- **Transcribed:** <YYYY-MM-DD> -> Explanations/PT<NNN>-explanations.md (<done> done, <skipped> skipped)` and refresh the file\'s `updated:` frontmatter. The shared ledger then shows both halves of a PT\'s lifecycle: built AND transcribed.\\r\\n- Report a short final summary: questions done, skips, flags.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Do NOT\\r\\n- Do not capture only one style when a style selector EXISTS — capture all three (Standard, Brief, Another Way). The only time one block is correct is a genuine single-style question with no selector (verify, then use `### Written (single style)` + the callout note). Never fabricate three styles to fill the template.\\r\\n- Do not make per-question files (one file per test, always).\\r\\n- Do not paraphrase, \\"improve\\", or fabricate percentages/tags/explanations.\\r\\n- Do not click \\"Request Written\\" (generates non-authored content).\\r\\n- Do not open a new tab or re-navigate when Joshua already has the set open; reuse his tab, matched by URL.\\r\\n- Do not use em dashes in your own prose (the answer-choice delimiter, and any real em dash inside a verbatim stimulus, are the only exceptions).\\r\\n- Do not stop for routine questions mid-run; pause only for genuine blockers (missing data, extension disconnect, RC-format decision, section-coverage confirmation).\\r\\n"}'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '{"chunk_id":"ea8e14","wall_time_seconds":1.489858,"exit_code":0,"original_token_count":90,"output":"skills\\\\codex\\\\_project-forks\\\\lsatu\\\\notebooklm\\\\CLAUDE.md\\nskills\\\\codex\\\\_project-forks\\\\lsatu\\\\notebooklm\\\\AGENTS.md\\nskills\\\\reference\\\\open-design\\\\AGENTS.md\\nskills\\\\claude\\\\notebooklm\\\\AGENTS.md\\nskills\\\\codex\\\\notebooklm\\\\AGENTS.md\\nskills\\\\claude\\\\notebooklm\\\\CLAUDE.md\\nskills\\\\codex\\\\notebooklm\\\\CLAUDE.md\\nskills\\\\gemini\\\\notebooklm\\\\CLAUDE.md\\nskills\\\\gemini\\\\notebooklm\\\\AGENTS.md\\n"}'}]

Capture only the selected lecture(s); this does not authorize library-wide collection or publishing. This skill handles video lectures; `lsat-lab-transcribe` handles written PrepTest explanations.\n+\n+## Capture once, then assemble\n+\n+1. Read the repository instructions and `wiki/teaching-voice.md`. Use the current approved browser skill and its documented tools. Reuse the selected LSAT Lab tab. In Codex, use CUA with the in-app browser; in another runtime, use its approved browser surface. Do not copy outdated CDP/Playwright instructions from older skills or guess media/caption URLs.\n+2. Verify the live title, instructor, lecture date, video ID, duration, and URL. Read the existing archive/index before creating anything. Home: `corpus/lectures/lsat-lab/<topic>/<lecture-date>-<videoid>/`. If the lecture date is unavailable, use `undated-<videoid>` and record that uncertainty.\n+3. Prefer the native full transcript/caption export, using supported UI/documented browser access. Preserve one full timestamped extraction and note whether captions are automated or authored. Check the opening, ending, and gaps against playback. If no captions exist, use an authorized audio transcription route and verify uncertain words against the video. Never replace missing source with a guessed lecture. Report incomplete access precisely.\n+4. Derive a chronological outline from the transcript, then seek to visual milestones. Capture the introductory concept board before the first question; each question's readable stimulus, stem, and all answer choices; and important intermediate/final board states showing the reasoning. Multiple frames are appropriate if the whole question cannot fit. Capture visuals where they explain a teaching step, not on an arbitrary every-N-seconds schedule. Verify the settled player timestamp and image readability after every seek.\n+5. Checkpoint `progress.json` after each completed segment/question: selected URL, duration, transcript status, verified through-time, completed question IDs, next seek time, missing captures, and blockers. Resume from this checkpoint instead of extracting everything again.\n+6. Create the adapted script in short, readable paragraphs. Keep nearly all substantive wording, examples, distinctions, wrong-answer reasoning, transitions, and teaching sequence. Remove student names, attendance, greetings, technical interruptions, repetition caused by interruption, and logistics. Replace name-based references with a natural direct explanation. Preserve specific question reasoning when it teaches something useful; do not collapse it into a generic concept summary. Mark uncertain words and missing material explicitly. Keep the untouched source transcript separate.\n+7. Map each script segment to its source time interval and transcript line interval, concept tags, question ID where applicable, and exact screenshot filenames/times. Put unspoken visual cues outside spoken text. The user should be able to read a paragraph, show the adjacent board, and continue without reconstructing the lesson. Include pause/check-for-understanding cues when supported by the source.\n+8. Use [the manifest contract](references/manifest.md) and `scripts/package_lecture.py` to generate `teleprompter.md`, self-contained `teleprompter.html`, `timeline.json`, `coverage.md`, `PROVENANCE.md`, and `SHA256SUMS`. The helper packages local captures only. It does not obtain captions or screenshots, judge logical correctness, or certify completeness. Preserve initial `transcript.md` and source screenshots unchanged; a later correction needs explicit provenance, not silent overwriting.\n+9. Review coverage against the entire source: all key teaching points, concept-board explanation, every worked question and substantive answer elimination, and conclusion. Record removed chatter and unresolved gaps. Check screenshot legibility and the script/image/time mapping. Open the HTML and test navigation, start/pause, speed, manual reading, and keyboard controls. Record actual checks; a successful helper run alone is not content verification.\n+10. Register the lecture/topic and concept tags in the owning lecture index, then append the meaningful system change to `wiki/log.md` following local routing. Report paths, source/caption confidence, questions and boards captured, and gaps. Never say complete when material or verification is missing. Do not send, post, or assign from this skill.\n+\n+## Read-aloud deliverable\n+\n+Keep the reading surface large and uncluttered, with navigation by concept/question, each screenshot beside the corresponding script, and timestamp cues visually distinct from speech. Autoscroll is optional and initially paused; respect reduced-motion preferences. The untouched transcript is evidence, the teleprompter is the cleaned reading copy, and coverage is the reconciliation between them.\n*** Add File: C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os/.agents/skills/lsat-lab-lecture-transcribe/references/manifest.md\n+# Local package contract\n+\n+Run with Python 3, no third-party libraries:\n+\n+```powershell\n+python .agents/skills/lsat-lab-lecture-transcribe/scripts/package_lecture.py --manifest <manifest.json> --transcript <captured-transcript.txt> --output <lecture-folder>\n+```\n+\n+The transcript is copied byte-for-byte into `transcript.md` on initial preservation. It can contain timestamps; use its actual physical lines in `source_lines`. If an existing source differs, the helper stops. Screenshots must already exist beneath the output folder's `screenshots/` directory. The output folder must be dedicated to this lecture.\n+\n+```json\n+{\n+  \"title\": \"Example lecture\",\n+  \"instructor\": \"Verified instructor\",\n+  \"lecture_date\": \"2026-08-27\",\n+  \"video_id\": \"33013\",\n+  \"source_url\": \"https://app.lsatlab.com/video-library/?videoid=33013\",\n+  \"duration_seconds\": 3600,\n+  \"capture_date\": \"2026-09-30\",\n+  \"transcript_method\": \"native automated captions, checked against playback\",\n+  \"source_complete\": false,\n+  \"verification_notes\": [\"Opening and ending checked; remaining review pending\"],\n+  \"gaps\": [\"Question 2 final board not yet captured\"],\n+  \"omissions\": [\"Greetings and student names removed from reading script\"],\n+  \"teaching_points\": [{\"id\": \"linking\", \"description\": \"Link compatible conditional rules\", \"segment_ids\": [\"concept-1\"]}],\n+  \"segments\": [{\n+    \"id\": \"concept-1\", \"title\": \"Link the rules\", \"kind\": \"concept\",\n+    \"start_seconds\": 60, \"end_seconds\": 120,\n+    \"source_lines\": [3, 8], \"concepts\": [\"conditional-linking\"],\n+    \"script\": \"First, look for the term that the two rules share.\\n\\nThat term gives us the connection.\",\n+    \"cues\": [\"Pause for the student to identify the shared term\"],\n+    \"images\": [{\"path\": \"screenshots/01-concept-board.png\", \"at_seconds\": 95, \"kind\": \"concept_board\", \"caption\": \"Initial linking board\"}]\n+  }]\n+}\n+```\n+\n+Segment `kind`: `concept`, `question`, or `closing`. Question segments require `question_id`. Image `kind`: `concept_board`, `question`, or `annotated_board`. Every question ID requires at least one `question` image; capture all text even if this needs several images. The first question must be preceded by a `concept_board` image in a concept segment. Image timestamps must fall within their segment. Split long explanations into separate segments if helpful. Segment times must be chronological and non-overlapping; deliberate transcript omissions are listed separately. Each teaching point must map to existing segments. Coverage proves referenced points have mappings, not that an agent identified every point.\n+\n+Use `source_complete: false` plus specific gaps while capture is unfinished. Verification notes must say what was actually checked. The helper rejects missing/misplaced images, invalid source ranges, unmapped teaching points, and inconsistent timestamps before writing deliverables. It checks previous source hashes before repackaging. Derived files can be regenerated; preserved source cannot be silently changed.\n*** End Patch"));

text(await tools.apply_patch("*** Begin Patch\n*** Add File: C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os/.agents/skills/lsat-lab-lecture-transcribe/scripts/package_lecture.py\n+\"\"\"Package already captured lecture material. No browser or network access.\"\"\"\n+import argparse\n+import base64\n+import hashlib\n+import html\n+import json\n+import mimetypes\n+from pathlib import Path\n+\n+\n+def stamp(value):\n+    seconds = int(value)\n+    return f\"{seconds // 3600:02}:{seconds // 60 % 60:02}:{seconds % 60:02}\"\n+\n+\n+def digest(data):\n+    return hashlib.sha256(data).hexdigest()\n+\n+\n+def validate(manifest, transcript, output):\n+    for key in (\"title\", \"source_url\", \"duration_seconds\", \"transcript_method\", \"capture_date\", \"source_complete\"):\n+        if key not in manifest:\n+            raise ValueError(f\"Missing manifest field: {key}\")\n+    if not isinstance(manifest[\"source_complete\"], bool):\n+        raise ValueError(\"source_complete must be a boolean\")\n+    duration = manifest[\"duration_seconds\"]\n+    if not isinstance(duration, (int, float)) or duration <= 0:\n+        raise ValueError(\"Invalid lecture duration\")\n+    segments = manifest.get(\"segments\", [])\n+    if not segments:\n+        raise ValueError(\"No captured script segments\")\n+    lines = len(transcript.decode(\"utf-8-sig\").splitlines())\n+    ids, questions, pictured = set(), set(), set()\n+    previous_end, initial_board = 0, False\n+    assets = {}\n+    for segment in segments:\n+        identifier = segment[\"id\"]\n+        if not isinstance(identifier, str) or not identifier or identifier in ids:\n+            raise ValueError(\"Segment IDs must be unique nonempty strings\")\n+        ids.add(identifier)\n+        start, end = segment[\"start_seconds\"], segment[\"end_seconds\"]\n+        if not 0 <= previous_end <= start < end <= duration:\n+            raise ValueError(f\"Invalid/overlapping source times: {identifier}\")\n+        previous_end = end\n+        first_line, last_line = segment[\"source_lines\"]\n+        if not 1 <= first_line <= last_line <= lines:\n+            raise ValueError(f\"Invalid transcript line range: {identifier}\")\n+        if not segment.get(\"script\", \"\").strip():\n+            raise ValueError(f\"Empty spoken script: {identifier}\")\n+        if segment[\"kind\"] not in {\"concept\", \"question\", \"closing\"}:\n+            raise ValueError(f\"Invalid segment kind: {identifier}\")\n+        if segment[\"kind\"] == \"question\":\n+            question = segment.get(\"question_id\")\n+            if not question or not initial_board:\n+                raise ValueError(\"Questions need question_id and an earlier introductory concept board\")\n+            questions.add(question)\n+        for frame in segment.get(\"images\", []):\n+            relative = Path(frame[\"path\"])\n+            path = (output / relative).resolve()\n+            if relative.is_absolute() or not path.is_relative_to((output / \"screenshots\").resolve()):\n+                raise ValueError(f\"Image must stay beneath screenshots/: {relative}\")\n+            if path.suffix.lower() not in {\".png\", \".jpg\", \".jpeg\", \".webp\"} or not path.is_file():\n+                raise ValueError(f\"Missing or unsupported image: {relative}\")\n+            if not start <= frame[\"at_seconds\"] <= end:\n+                raise ValueError(f\"Image time outside segment: {relative}\")\n+            if frame[\"kind\"] not in {\"concept_board\", \"question\", \"annotated_board\"}:\n+                raise ValueError(f\"Invalid image kind: {relative}\")\n+            if frame[\"kind\"] == \"concept_board\" and segment[\"kind\"] == \"concept\":\n+                initial_board = True\n+            if frame[\"kind\"] == \"question\" and segment[\"kind\"] == \"question\":\n+                pictured.add(segment[\"question_id\"])\n+            assets[relative.as_posix()] = path.read_bytes()\n+    if questions != pictured:\n+        raise ValueError(f\"Question screenshots missing: {sorted(questions - pictured)}\")\n+    if not initial_board:\n+        raise ValueError(\"Initial concept board is missing\")\n+    for point in manifest.get(\"teaching_points\", []):\n+        if not point.get(\"segment_ids\") or not set(point[\"segment_ids\"]) <= ids:\n+            raise ValueError(f\"Unmapped teaching point: {point.get('id')}\")\n+    if manifest[\"source_complete\"] and manifest.get(\"gaps\"):\n+        raise ValueError(\"A complete source cannot have unresolved gaps\")\n+    return assets\n+\n+\n+STYLE = \"\"\"body{margin:0;background:#f7f4ed;color:#172d35;font-family:Georgia,serif}header{position:sticky;top:0;background:#fff;padding:12px 24px;z-index:2;border-bottom:1px solid id=\"run\">Start</button><label>Speed <input id=\"speed\" type=\"range\" min=\"5\" max=\"90\" value=\"24\" aria-label=\"Scroll pixels per second\"></label><span id=\"status\" [{'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'Script completed\nWall time 3.4 seconds\nOutput:\n'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '{"chunk_id":"692aad","wall_time_seconds":1.5055817999999999,"exit_code":0,"original_token_count":2481,"output":"{\\r\\n  \\"title\\": \\"Must Be True\\",\\r\\n  \\"segments\\": [\\r\\n    {\\r\\n      \\"id\\": \\"concept-1\\",\\r\\n      \\"title\\": \\"Accept the facts and learn the three conditional moves\\",\\r\\n      \\"kind\\": \\"concept\\",\\r\\n      \\"start_seconds\\": 2.3,\\r\\n      \\"end_seconds\\": 1092,\\r\\n      \\"source_lines\\": [\\r\\n        1,\\r\\n        307\\r\\n      ],\\r\\n      \\"concepts\\": [\\r\\n        \\"inference-mindset\\",\\r\\n        \\"contrapositive\\",\\r\\n        \\"conditional-linking\\",\\r\\n        \\"fact-triggers-rule\\",\\r\\n        \\"illegal-reversal\\",\\r\\n        \\"illegal-negation\\",\\r\\n        \\"conditional-certainty\\",\\r\\n        \\"inference-checklist\\"\\r\\n      ],\\r\\n      \\"cues\\": [\\r\\n        \\"Show the opening concept board.\\",\\r\\n        \\"Pause after each rhetorical question.\\",\\r\\n        \\"Show the two chaining patterns and their completed contrapositives.\\",\\r\\n        \\"Show the list of inference clues.\\"\\r\\n      ],\\r\\n      \\"images\\": [],\\r\\n      \\"script\\": \\"We\'re talking about Must Be True. We might come across Must Be False at the same time, because both are in the inference family.\\\\n\\\\nWhen we think about the inference family, we\'re thinking: I\'m accepting the information I\'m reading. I\'m not fighting a conclusion or looking for an assumption. That\'s a different mindset from much of the test. Here, you accept the supplied facts, but it may be unclear what to do with them. They\'re sitting there as information, without necessarily giving you an opinion to evaluate.\\\\n\\\\nMust Be True and Must Be False have these nauseating difficulty curves. There are so many level-three, level-four, and level-five questions, and very few level-one questions. Why? The instructor\'s explanation is that these types are dominated by conditional logic. These questions can feel difficult because so many of them involve conditional logic. If conditional logic is a stumbling block, it\'s worth working on directly. Flashcards and conditional rephrasing are useful: look at a statement, then turn it into conditional form. Memorize the rules until using them becomes automatic. In the end, these questions are very doable. You just have to acquire a skill that people don\'t normally have. Conditional logic isn\'t necessarily an intuitive skill you brought into the LSAT. You acquire it through practice.\\\\n\\\\nHere\'s what they\'re testing us on. First and foremost: contrapositives. Look at the statement on the board. What is its contrapositive?\\\\n\\\\nEverything switches sides, and everything switches signs. That includes changing AND to OR, or OR to AND, when you negate a combined condition. You\'re reversing the direction and negating the complete conditions. That\'s the move. In the board example, A leads to not B and C. Negate that entire result: B or not C. Then reverse the arrow. The correct contrapositive is B or not C leads to not A. The original board drops the not before C; keep that negative in your version.\\\\n\\\\nNumber two: chain conditionals together. What\'s the rule for when you can and can\'t chain them?\\\\n\\\\nYou need the same idea in opposite positions: the necessary condition of one rule is the sufficient condition of the next. If A leads to B, and B leads to C, the overlapping B goes in the middle. You can write the whole chain, A to B to C, or take the express lane from A to C. You can contrapose the entire chain by reversing the order and negating each idea.\\\\n\\\\nThere\'s another presentation that hides the same connection. Look at A to B and not A to C. Here we have opposite ideas, A and not A, in the same position: both are triggers. How can these connect?\\\\n\\\\nTry contraposing either rule. If we contrapose the bottom rule, not A to C becomes not C to A. Now the connection is visible: not C to A to B. As soon as you contrapose it, you see the familiar common term in the middle.\\\\n\\\\nThe original presentation takes longer to process. Your brain doesn\'t see what you\'re supposed to do. With the overlapping idea in opposite positions, we immediately think: put that idea in the middle, and we\'ve got ourselves a chain. With opposite ideas in the same position, that connection doesn\'t jump out at us. That\'s why the contrapositive matters. Sometimes you have to do something to reveal the connection.\\\\n\\\\nIf we have the same idea in the same position, that doesn\'t establish this kind of chain. A to B and A to C can be consolidated: A gives us both B and C. But we don\'t get B to C, or C to B. Likewise, A to B and C to B share a result; contraposing both shows that not B leads to both not A and not C. That still doesn\'t create a relationship between A and C.\\\\n\\\\nWhen we have a chain, we\'re often looking from the beginning to the end. But don\'t become attached to the longest possible inference. The instructor observes that older tests often reward the full head-to-tail inference, while more recent questions may hide the result by testing a shorter stretch. If there\'s a four-part chain, an answer can test A to C or B to D rather than A to D. This is a test-taking observation, not a limit on which inferences are valid. You need to be comfortable with those shorter connections too. A diagram helps: you can look over and see that a proposed connection is available without reconstructing all the wording.\\\\n\\\\nThe third skill is seeing when a fact triggers a rule. If I tell you Bob is X, and give you a rule saying X leads to Y, what can you infer? Bob is Y. That feels very easy in symbols.\\\\n\\\\nNow suppose the rule is A to B, but Pam is not B. What can we infer? Pam is not A. All we\'ve done is use the contrapositive. The negative fact didn\'t look like the trigger of the original rule, but it triggers the contrapositive.\\\\n\\\\nNotice how the contrapositive comes back in both situations. It can hide a chain, and it can hide the fact that we\'re activating a rule. Rephrasing the rule reveals the simple move underneath.\\\\n\\\\nShould we always write the contrapositive of every rule? You could make a case either way. It takes time, and sometimes the original rules are already straightforward. If I can see A to B and B to C, I don\'t need a second set of diagrams to connect them.\\\\n\\\\nHere\'s a practical approach: if you\'re staring at conditional logic and don\'t know what to do, contrapose one of the rules. That will often make it click. If you already know what to do, writing every contrapositive may be unnecessary.\\\\n\\\\nFor learning, though, the standard is different. If it takes you a second to produce a contrapositive, even when you get it right, keep practising. Suppose I say, only poets can date French women. Only points at poets, so dating French women requires being a poet. The contrapositive is: if you\'re not a poet, you can\'t date French women under that rule. You want that translation to be immediate.\\\\n\\\\nIf you\'re newer to conditional logic, writing the contrapositive each time can be useful until it becomes automatic. You do enough repetitions that the move stops taking your attention. Then you can spend your attention on what the passage means.\\\\n\\\\nIs that all of Must Be True? No. But conditional reasoning is the main emphasis of this lesson. We also have to learn what not to do.\\\\n\\\\nFirst, resist chaining statements that don\'t connect. A to B and A to C don\'t give you B to C. An answer choice may tempt you with exactly that invented relationship.\\\\n\\\\nThen resist illegal reversal and illegal negation. If you\'re in the NBA, you\'re rich. Does that mean if you\'re rich, you\'re in the NBA? No. That reverses the rule. Does it mean if you\'re not in the NBA, you\'re not rich? No. That negates without reversing. Other people can be rich.\\\\n\\\\nThe legal contrapositive would be: if you\'re not rich, you\'re not in the NBA. Reverse and negate. We have to make the difference between those moves automatic.\\\\n\\\\nIt\'s easier with simple letters. It gets harder with long sentences about science or morality. Let\'s practise finding the conditional wording in actual paragraphs.\\\\n\\\\nOnly is a clue. All is a clue. If is a clue. But the keyword alone doesn\'t settle it.\\\\n\\\\nSuppose a sentence says, when public water supplies are drawn from aquifers, chlorine is often added. When sounds conditional, but look at the main clause: often added. That isn\'t a guaranteed result. We can\'t treat it as an all-or-nothing conditional.\\\\n\\\\nAs a result of is causal wording. If the statement says contamination can occur as a result of something, can occur doesn\'t provide a guaranteed result either.\\\\n\\\\nWhat about some? Some clowns wear rubber noses establishes at least one case, but it doesn\'t say all clowns wear them. Some and most do not by themselves give us a universal conditional connecting the entire group. All and none can.\\\\n\\\\nConditionals don\'t always have keywords. Suppose the statement says exact caffeine content isn\'t listed on soft-drink labels. That\'s presented as a statement about the whole class of those labels. We can read it as: if it\'s a soft-drink label, it doesn\'t list exact caffeine content. A universal noun can carry the relationship even when there\'s no if.\\\\n\\\\nAnother sentence says listing exact caffeine content would make it easier to limit caffeine intake. This is causal in what it describes: the listing makes something easier. It\'s also presented as a definite relationship: if you list the exact content, it would be easier to limit intake. A statement can have both causal and conditional features.\\\\n\\\\nFor inference questions generally, here\'s the bigger list of clues: conditional relationships, causal relationships, pivot words creating tension, numerical information, and implied similarities or differences.\\\\n\\\\nFor Must Be True, immediately consider conditional logic. For Most Strongly Supported, causal relationships often deserve attention. But don\'t ignore the other clues. A passage may be asking you to combine numbers or reconcile a contrast. Start with the relationships that are actually there, then ask what they support.\\"\\r\\n    },\\r\\n    {\\r\\n      \\"id\\": \\"question-1\\",\\r\\n      \\"title\\": \\"University bookstores: a Exact question text requires screenshots; read prompts refer to displayed original rather than fabricate absent wording.\', \'Clear spoken/ASR logical slips corrected in adapted narrator text and listed below; script is an adaptation, not a verbatim quotation.\', \'Source ranges use physical cue-line numbers in source-transcript.txt.\', \'Fidelity enhancement preserves additional original examples and explanation: difficulty discussion, opposite-position recognition, partial-chain observation, bookstore repeated-term rationale, case-by-case diagramming, until/context caution, pigeon-label analogy, measurement prediction, passive-voice example. Q4 C specific restriction added from parent screenshot extraction; exact wording still to verify against image.\', \'Full native caption file captured through 96:54 of 97:00 runtime; automated speech recognition remains unverified against all audio.\', \'Parent visually checked initial board and full stimulus/stem/all answer choices for five worked questions.\', \'Original board negation error and Q2 modifier-scope conflict explicitly corrected in reading script and image captions.\']\\r\\nquickly reject faces that don\'t resemble the person you\'re seeking, and slow down when you see a possible match. This isn\'t how every question works, but sometimes the inference is clear enough to search for it directly.\\r\\n\\r\\nE is the answer. Its wording is softer than our prediction. At least some universities fail in at least one of the relevant responsibilities. That\'s a consequence of the broader failure we derived. The answer doesn\'t have to repeat our prediction word for word or preserve its full strength. A weaker statement can follow from a stronger one.\\r\\n\\r\\nNow check the tempting alternatives. C talks about most people who purchase publications at university bookstores. Where did our most statement apply? To university bookstores, not to purchasers.\\r\\n\\r\\nThose are different groups. Most stores having a particular bestseller doesn\'t establish what most individual buyers do. You have to be able to derive the answer from the paragraph. For this answer to say most people who purchase publications, we would need some information supporting a majority of those people. The majority claim we actually received was about stores. You can\'t change the group simply because those people are involved in the stores. The passage hasn\'t supplied a relationship that lets us change the denominator from stores to people. That is why this most claim cannot be derived here.\\r\\n\\r\\nD resembles E in some of its wording, but look at the subject. Our rule is about universities. D shifts to the people who attend them. A statement about the institution isn\'t automatically a statement about its students. Keep the subject of the inference attached to the subject of the rule.\\r\\n\\r\\nThe useful checks here are simple. Does the fact activate the original rule or its contrapositive? Does the answer preserve the relevant group? Does it say something no stronger than what we can actually derive? E passes those checks.\\r\\n"}'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '{}'}]

text(await tools.apply_patch("*** Begin Patch\n*** Add File: C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os/corpus/lectures/logical-reasoning/must-be-true/core/2026-08-27-33013/teaching-brief.md\n+# Before teaching: Must Be True\n+\n+This is unspoken preparation for the shared lecture library. Open the [teleprompter](teleprompter.html) when ready to teach. Source: Patrick Tyrrell's LSAT Lab lecture, 2026-08-27, video 33013. Runtime: 97 minutes. The script follows the whole lecture, with five worked questions and an introductory scan of additional examples.\n+\n+## What the lesson teaches\n+\n+Accept the supplied facts. Make only the connections those facts support. The three central moves are contraposing a rule, linking compatible rules, and using a fact to trigger a rule or its contrapositive. Preserve the subject, quantifier, and strength of the conclusion. The lesson also introduces inference pivots, numerical boundaries, requirement language, passive voice, and temporal reasoning. Q4 is Most Strongly Supported, although the lecture is filed under Must Be True.\n+\n+## What to check before starting\n+\n+Check that the student can distinguish sufficient from necessary conditions, translate only, reverse and negate for contraposition, and negate AND as OR. If those operations take effort, pause on the opening board. Do not make familiarity with every advanced example a prerequisite; use the examples to expose gaps.\n+\n+## Follow the teaching sequence\n+\n+1. Opening concepts, 00:02 to 18:12. Start with the completed board at 06:05, then the inference checklist at 12:43. Teach the three moves and illegal reversal/negation. The lipid-profile display at 17:45 is an introductory scanning example, not one of the five full worked questions.\n+2. Q1, PT8 S1 Q19, 18:12 to 28:56. University bookstores: a fact activates a contrapositive. Show the full question at 24:50 and the rule board at 23:20. Check that the result preserves universities as its subject and does not replace a claim about most stores with most purchasers.\n+3. Pivot concepts, 28:56 to 33:25.8. The 32:35 board organizes comparison, contest, unfair situation, and paradox. Identify the relationship the passage supplies before hunting for an answer.\n+4. Q2, PT14 S2 Q2, 33:25.8 to 43:09.6. Applicants and manager requirements: show the stem at 33:45 and full choices at 34:23. Keep the NOT-meet stem visible. Apply each applicant's facts to the actual requirements; the listed relevant fields are sociology, psychology, and education.\n+5. Q3, PT113 S2 Q2, 43:09.6 to 54:54.4. Bridge testing: show the full question at 49:20 and measurement board at 48:27. Track the refusal standard, 24 blows, and two-inch result. Treat the definition and comparison carefully; choose only the supported strength.\n+6. Q4, PT10 S1 Q24, 54:54.4 to 76:26.8. Most Strongly Supported: show the full question at 62:40 and antibody-production timeline at 60:50. Keep the timeline separate from claims about all defenses. C says antibodies are produced only for viral infections that cannot be fought by any other body defenses; that exclusivity is not established.\n+7. Q5, PT121 S1 Q26, 76:26.8 to 96:23.8. Language and verbal signs: show the full question at 85:10 and chain at 83:55. Parse the relative clause and passive voice, link rules, and check the direction of every answer. Silence about an outcome does not establish its opposite.\n+8. Closing, 96:23.8 to 96:54.6. Consolidate the three moves. Ask the student to name which move unlocked each question and which tempting inference went beyond the facts.\n+\n+The gallery is organized for use rather than strict screenshot-time order: the full question appears before its explanatory boards. Timestamps identify each source frame. Click an image to enlarge it; the script remains the reading copy.\n+\n+## Correct these source slips while teaching\n+\n+The opening board has A leading to not B AND C, but its written contrapositive omits a negative before C. Teach B OR not C leading to not A. The preserved image shows the source typo; its caption and script correct it.\n+\n+Q2's instructor verbally limits the relevant fields to the experience route. The displayed stimulus says these requirements. The adapted script follows the displayed question rather than that spoken restriction.\n+\n+For Q1, negating fulfillment of both moral and intellectual responsibilities does not establish failure of both. At least one responsibility fails. Do not turn that into every university failing both responsibilities.\n+\n+Other caption or spoken slips are itemized in [coverage.md](coverage.md). Read that ledger if a source line and reading script appear to disagree.\n+\n+## Use the script without losing the reasoning\n+\n+Keep autoscroll paused while showing a question. Read the question with the student, ask for a prediction, and continue the worked explanation. Pause at each conditional transformation long enough for the student to explain why it is legal. Use the adjacent timestamp buttons to select the board relevant to that part of the explanation. You can navigate directly to a question when revisiting a skill; the full source sequence remains available.\n+\n+The complete native automated captions were captured, but the entire lecture has not been audited against audio. The script is a cleaned adaptation with explicit logical corrections. Browser navigation, galleries, zoom, and scrolling controls were checked. Keyboard controls are implemented but remain unverified after a browser focus-test failure.\n*** End Patch"));

{segment[\"id\"]: segment for segment in manifest[\"segments\"]}\n+    questions = {}\n+    for question in lesson.get(\"question_bank\", []):\n+        identifier = question.get(\"id\", {})\n+        if isinstance(choices, list):\n+            choices = {choice[\"label\"]: choice[\"text\"] for choice in choices}\n+        if set(choices) != set(\"ABCDE\") or not question.get(\"stimulus\") or not question.get(\"stem\"):\n+            raise ValueError(f\"Question {identifier} needs stimulus, stem and all five choices\")\n+        questions[identifier] = {**question, \"choices\": choices}\n+    cards, assets, ids = [], {}, set()\n+    for item in lesson.get(\"steps\", []):\n+        identifier = item[\"id\"]\n+        if identifier in ids or kind not in {\"say\", \"ask\", \"wait\", \"expected\", \"board\", \"question\"}:\n+            raise ValueError(f\"Invalid teaching step: {identifier}\")\n+        step = dict(item)\n+        if kind != \"question\" and not str(step.get(\"text\", \"\")).strip():\n+            raise ValueError(f\"Empty teaching step: {identifier}\")\n+        if kind == \"say\" and len(step[\"text\"].split()) > 100:\n+            raise ValueError(f\"SAY step exceeds 100 words; split it into short teaching moments: {identifier}\")\n+        if step.get(\"question_id\"):\n+            if step[\"question_id\"] not in questions:\n+                raise ValueError(f\"Unresolved question: {identifier}\")\n+            step[\"question\"] = questions[step[\"question_id\"]]\n+        if kind == \"question\" and \"screenshots\").resolve()) or not path.is_file():\n+                raise ValueError(f\"Missing or misplaced teaching image: {image}\")\n+            data = path.read_bytes()\n+            assets[Path(image).as_posix()] = data\n+            mime = mimetypes.guess_type(image)[0] or \"image/png\"\n+            step[\"image\"] = step[\"type\"] == \"question\"}\n+    if not expected_questions <= shown_questions:\n+        raise ValueError(\"Every worked question needs its complete question teaching card\")\n+    for [{'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'Script completed\nWall time 1.7 seconds\nOutput:\n'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '{"chunk_id":"a13647","wall_time_seconds":1.5515623,"exit_code":0,"original_token_count":901,"output":"{\'id\': \'step-050\', \'section_id\': \'concept-1\', \'type\': \'say\', \'title\': \'Accept the facts and learn the three conditional moves\', \'text\': \\"For inference questions generally, here\'s the bigger list of clues: conditional relationships, causal relationships, pivot words creating tension, numerical information, and implied similarities or differences.\\"}\\r\\n{\'id\': \'step-051\', \'section_id\': \'concept-1\', \'type\': \'say\', \'title\': \'Accept the facts and learn the three conditional moves\', \'text\': \\"For Must Be True, immediately consider conditional logic.\\\\n\\\\nFor Most Strongly Supported, causal relationships often deserve attention.\\\\n\\\\nBut don\'t ignore the other clues.\\\\n\\\\nA passage may be asking you to combine numbers or reconcile a contrast.\\\\n\\\\nStart with the relationships that are actually there, then ask what they support.\\"}\\r\\n{\'id\': \'step-052\', \'section_id\': \'question-1\', \'type\': \'question\', \'title\': \'PT8 S1 Q19\', \'text\': \'Read the original stimulus, stem, and all five choices. Keep the question visible during the walkthrough.\', \'question_id\': \'Q1\', \'image_path\': \'screenshots/extra-1490.png\'}\\r\\n{\'id\': \'step-054\', \'section_id\': \'question-1\', \'type\': \'wait\', \'title\': \'Give them time to work\', \'text\': \'Give the student time to work on the question independently first. Start the two-minute timer as a suggested pace, or pause it if more time is useful. When finished, ask for their reasoning.\', \'question_id\': \'Q1\', \'duration_seconds\': 120, \'pacing_note\': \'Suggested facilitation pace, not a timing value stated in the source lecture.\'}\\r\\n{\'id\': \'step-053\', \'section_id\': \'question-1\', \'type\': \'ask\', \'title\': \'Ask the student\', \'text\': \'When you are finished, what relationship or boundary did you notice? What did you predict, and how did you test the answers?\', \'question_id\': \'Q1\'}\\r\\n{\'id\': \'step-055\', \'section_id\': \'question-1\', \'type\': \'expected\', \'title\': \'Expected response\', \'text\': \'Identify the actual rule, fact, chain, qualification checklist, or numerical boundary. Keep the prediction provisional; do not add information.\', \'question_id\': \'Q1\'}\\r\\n{\'id\': \'step-056\', \'section_id\': \'question-1\', \'type\': \'say\', \'title\': \'University bookstores: a fact activates the contrapositive\', \'text\': \\"Read the university-bookstore question on screen.\\\\n\\\\nBefore looking at the answers, ask whether we\'re contraposing, chaining, or using a fact to activate a rule.\\", \'question_id\': \'Q1\'}\\r\\n{\'id\': \'step-057\', \'section_id\': \'question-1\', \'type\': \'say\', \'title\': \'University bookstores: a fact activates the contrapositive\', \'text\': \\"The first sentence is definitely conditional.\\\\n\\\\nThe second information also uses only.\\\\n\\\\nYou could make that conditional, but first ask what the passage is asking you to do with it.\\\\n\\\\nThe last information brings back TV Today and Gossip Review, so those repeated ideas should catch your attention.\\\\n\\\\nThat repeated term is a great instinct to follow.\\\\n\\\\nWe\'re talking about most university bookstores and these two magazines again.\\\\n\\\\nIt feels like the two sentences are designed to interact.\\", \'question_id\': \'Q1\'}\\r\\n{\'id\': \'step-058\', \'section_id\': \'question-1\', \'type\': \'say\', \'title\': \'University bookstores: a fact activates the contrapositive\', \'text\': \\"The final information tells us that, in most university bookstores, these two magazines occupy the top positions.\\\\n\\\\nThat establishes that they\'re the bestselling publications in those stores.\\\\n\\\\nWe could represent some of the wording with another conditional, but we don\'t need to diagram every detail.\\\\n\\\\nWhat matters is that the fact supplies the condition used by the first rule.\\", \'question_id\': \'Q1\'}\\r\\n"}'}]

Autoscroll is optional and initially paused; respect reduced-motion preferences. The untouched transcript is evidence, the teleprompter is the cleaned reading copy, and coverage is the reconciliation between them.\n+The default `teleprompter.html` is a private teacher cue sheet, one teaching moment at a time. Use short SAY chunks of roughly 40-80 spoken words, clear exact ASK wording, separate WAIT instructions, hidden EXPECTED RESPONSE, and copyable plain-ASCII BOARD content. Show each complete QUESTION's stimulus, stem, and five choices, with its original frame available to enlarge. Copy-question text must exclude answers and teacher notes. Put a suggested editable 120-second work timer before each question's walkthrough; identify this as teacher pacing, not a claimed source timing. Start/Pause/Reset never advances automatically; the teacher continues when ready. Keep the related question or board alongside its Socratic prompts.\n+\n+Prepare sibling `lesson-steps.json` using [the contract](references/manifest.md). Preserve every substantive script passage across SAY steps, allowing only tracked voice substitutions. The helper verifies this coverage. It renders Previous/Next, section selection, step progress, copy buttons, optional text-size settings, and clipboard-selection fallback. The brief is closed by default and opens separately. Any optional SAY-only automatic pacing starts disabled and stops at every ASK, WAIT, BOARD, or QUESTION. The full continuous lecture remains in `teleprompter.md` and `continuous-script.html`; do not lose teaching detail to shorten the visible cards. This UI is for Joshua to read privately, not a student presentation page.\n*** Update File: C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os/.agents/skills/lsat-lab-lecture-transcribe/references/manifest.md\n@@\n Use `source_complete: false` plus specific gaps while capture is unfinished. Verification notes must say what was actually checked. The helper rejects missing/misplaced images, invalid source ranges, unmapped teaching points, and inconsistent timestamps before writing deliverables. It checks previous source hashes before repackaging. Derived files can be regenerated; preserved source cannot be silently changed.\n+\n+## Private teaching moments\n+\n+New teaching packages also contain sibling `lesson-steps.json`, which the helper automatically loads. Its `question_bank` is a list of `{id, stimulus, stem, choices, image_path}` entries. `choices` is an A-E object or a list of `{label,text}`. Verify exact text against original question frames before packaging; never reconstruct absent words from the spoken explanation.\n+\n+Its `steps` is an ordered list of `{id, section_id, type, title, text}`. Types are `say`, `ask`, `wait`, `expected`, `board`, and `question`. `section_id` references a manifest segment. Optional fields: `question_id`, `image_path`, `source_time`, and `spoken_intro`. QUESTION steps reference the exact question bank. BOARD text is plain ASCII for copying to a live board. SAY chunks target 40-80 words; the helper rejects chunks exceeding 100. Preserve the source script across consecutive SAY chunks, with natural line breaks. Additional ASK/WAIT/EXPECTED prompts must be accurate and marked as adapted facilitation rather than source quotations.\n+\n+Before every walkthrough, add a question-associated WAIT with `duration_seconds: 120` and a `pacing_note` explaining that it is a suggested teaching pace. The next ASK becomes a separate card after the timed wait, so the question remains visible during independent work. Expected responses are hidden until revealed.
