classroom-button-primary\\" type=\\"button\\" disabled>Ask</button><button class=\\"classroom-button\\" type=\\"button\\" disabled>Back to notes</button></div>\\r\\n  </div></main>\\r\\n</div>\\r\\n<script>\\r\\nif(new URLSearchParams(location.search).get(\'theme\')===\'dark\')document.documentElement.classList.add(\'dark\');\\r\\nconst slider=document.getElementById(\'quantity-slider\');\\r\\nconst count=document.getElementById(\'quantity-count\');\\r\\nconst books=[...document.querySelectorAll(\'.quantity-lab-book\')];\\r\\nconst rules={some:1,most:5,all:8};\\r\\nslider.addEventListener(\'input\',()=>{const value=Number(slider.value);count.textContent=value;books.forEach((book,index)=>{const was=book.classList.contains(\'is-hardcover\');const now=index<value;book.classList.toggle(\'is-hardcover\',now);if(now&&!was){book.classList.remove(\'is-new\');void book.offsetWidth;book.classList.add(\'is-new\');setTimeout(()=>book.classList.remove(\'is-new\'),700)}});for(const [rule,minimum] of Object.entries(rules)){const pill=document.querySelector(\'[data-rule=\\"\'+rule+\'\\"]\');const was=pill.classList.contains(\'is-true\');const now=value>=minimum;pill.classList.toggle(\'is-true\',now);if(now&&!was){pill.classList.remove(\'is-new\');void pill.offsetWidth;pill.classList.add(\'is-new\');setTimeout(()=>pill.classList.remove(\'is-new\'),600)}}});\\r\\nconst progress=document.querySelector(\'.quantity-progress\');let pending=false;\\r\\nfunction updateProgress(){const max=document.documentElement.scrollHeight-innerHeight;progress.style.transform=\'scaleX(\'+Math.max(0,Math.min(1,max>0?scrollY/max:0))+\')\';pending=false}\\r\\naddEventListener(\'scroll\',()=>{if(!pending){requestAnimationFrame(updateProgress);pending=true}},{passive:true});addEventListener(\'resize\',updateProgress);updateProgress();\\r\\nconst workerArrangements=[[4,5,6,7,8,9],[0,1,2,3,4,5],[2,3,4,5,6,7],[1,3,5,7,8,9]];let arrangement=0;\\r\\ndocument.getElementById(\'quantity-overlap-shuffle\').addEventListener(\'click\',()=>{arrangement=(arrangement+1)%workerArrangements.length;const working=new Set(workerArrangements[arrangement]);const people=[...document.querySelectorAll(\'.quantity-person\')];let overlap=0;people.forEach((person,index)=>{const both=index<6&&working.has(index);person.classList.toggle(\'works-part-time\',working.has(index));person.setAttribute(\'aria-label\',\'Member \'+(index+1)+\': \'+(index<6?\'studies law\':\'does not study law\')+\', \'+(working.has(index)?\'works part time\':\'does not work part time\'));if(both)overlap++});document.getElementById(\'quantity-overlap-count\').textContent=overlap;document.getElementById(\'quantity-overlap-message\').textContent=overlap===2?\'Even the smallest possible overlap is not zero.\':\'The overlap changed, but it did not disappear.\';document.getElementById(\'venn-law-only\').textContent=6-overlap;document.getElementById(\'venn-both\').textContent=overlap;document.getElementById(\'venn-work-only\').textContent=6-overlap;document.getElementById(\'venn-neither\').textContent=overlap-2});\\r\\nconst inlineCheckRules=${JSON.stringify(checkKeys.map(group => group.map(pattern => ({ source: pattern.source, flags: pattern.flags }))))};\\r\\ndocument.querySelectorAll(\'.quantity-check\').forEach(card=>{const form=card.querySelector(\'form\');const input=form.querySelector(\'textarea\');const result=card.querySelector(\'.quantity-check-result\');form.addEventListener(\'submit\',event=>{event.preventDefault();const response=input.value.trim();if(!response)return;const index=card.dataset.check;let matched=false;if(index!==undefined){matched=inlineCheckRules[Number(index)].every(pattern=>new RegExp(pattern.source,pattern.flags).test(response))}else{const drill=Number(card.dataset.drill);const lower=response.toLowerCase();matched=drill===0?lower.includes(\'1\')&&lower.includes(\'2\'):drill===1?lower.includes(\'3\')&&lower.includes(\'4\'):drill===2?[\'no\',\'not\',\'cannot\',\\"can\'t\\"].some(word=>lower.includes(word)):[\'yes\',\'can\',\'trained\',\'weekend\'].some(word=>lower.includes(word))}result.querySelector(\'strong\').textContent=matched?\'Your answer has the key idea. Check the reason below.\':\'Compare your answer with the worked reason.\';result.hidden=false;result.scrollIntoView({block:\'nearest\',behavior:matchMedia(\'(prefers-reduced-motion: reduce)\').matches?\'instant\':\'smooth\'});});card.querySelector(\'.quantity-check-again\').addEventListener(\'click\',()=>{result.hidden=true;input.focus()})});\\r\\nconst quizQuestions=${JSON.stringify(quizQuestions)};\\r\\nconst quizForm=document.getElementById(\'quantity-quiz-form\');\\r\\nconst quizInput=document.getElementById(\'quantity-quiz-input\');\\r\\nconst quizFeedback=document.getElementById(\'quantity-quiz-feedback\');\\r\\nconst quizStage=document.querySelector(\'.quantity-quiz-stage\');\\r\\nconst quizDone=document.getElementById(\'quantity-quiz-done\');\\r\\nconst quizBridge=document.getElementById(\'quantity-quiz-bridge\');\\r\\nlet quizIndex=0,quizMatches=0;\\r\\nfunction renderQuiz(){const q=quizQuestions[quizIndex];document.getElementById(\'quantity-quiz-progress\').textContent=String(quizIndex+1).padStart(2,\'0\')+\' / 07\';document.getElementById(\'quantity-quiz-track-fill\').style.width=((quizIndex+1)/quizQuestions.length*100)+\'%\';document.getElementById(\'quantity-quiz-premises\').replaceChildren(Object.assign(document.createElement(\'span\'),{textContent:\'Given\'}),...q.premises.map(line=>Object.assign(document.createElement(\'strong\'),{textContent:line})));quizInput.value=\'\';quizForm.hidden=false;quizFeedback.hidden=true;quizBridge.classList.remove(\'is-yes\',\'is-no\');document.getElementById(\'quantity-quiz-error\').textContent=\'\';}\\r\\nquizForm.addEventListener(\'submit\',event=>{event.preventDefault();const response=quizInput.value.trim();const choice=/^(yes|yeah|yep|no|not guaranteed|cannot|can\'t|does not|doesn\'t)(?=[^a-z]|$)/i.exec(response);if(!choice){document.getElementById(\'quantity-quiz-error\').textContent=\'Begin with Yes or No, then give your reason.\';quizInput.focus();return}const verdict=/^(yes|yeah|yep)/i.test(choice[1])?\'yes\':\'no\';const q=quizQuestions[quizIndex];const match=verdict===q.answer;if(match)quizMatches++;document.getElementById(\'quantity-quiz-error\').textContent=\'\';document.getElementById(\'quantity-quiz-feedback-label\').textContent=match?\'Your conclusion matches\':\'Your conclusion needs another look\';document.getElementById(\'quantity-quiz-verdict\').textContent=q.answer===\'yes\'?\'Yes. The overlap follows.\':\'No. The overlap is not guaranteed.\';document.getElementById(\'quantity-quiz-explanation\').textContent=q.explanation;document.getElementById(\'quantity-quiz-next\').innerHTML=quizIndex===quizQuestions.length-1?\'See your review <span aria-hidden=\\"true\\">→</span>\':\'Next question <span aria-hidden=\\"true\\">→</span>\';quizBridge.classList.add(q.answer===\'yes\'?\'is-yes\':\'is-no\');quizForm.hidden=true;quizFeedback.hidden=false;quizFeedback.focus();});\\r\\ndocument.getElementById(\'quantity-quiz-next\').addEventListener(\'click\',()=>{if(quizIndex===quizQuestions.length-1){quizStage.hidden=true;quizDone.hidden=false;document.getElementById(\'quantity-quiz-score\').textContent=quizMatches+\' of 7 conclusions matched\';document.getElementById(\'quantity-quiz-progress\').textContent=\'07 / 07\';}else{quizIndex++;renderQuiz();quizInput.focus()}});\\r\\ndocument.getElementById(\'quantity-quiz-restart\').addEventListener(\'click\',()=>{quizIndex=0;quizMatches=0;quizDone.hidden=true;quizStage.hidden=false;renderQuiz();quizInput.focus()});\\r\\n</script>\\r\\n</body></html>`;\\r\\n\\r\\nconst out = path.join(here, \\"08-quantity-statements-visual-preview.html\\");\\r\\nfs.writeFileSync(out, page, \\"utf8\\");\\r\\nconsole.log(out);\\r\\n"}'}]

border: 0; border-radius: 7px; min-height: 42px; padding: 9px 15px; background: #006d70; color: #fff; font: 700 14px \'Outfit\',sans-serif; margin-top: 12px; }\\r\\n  .classroom-root .quantity-note .quantity-check button:hover { background: #005658; }\\r\\n  .classroom-root .quantity-note .quantity-check button:focus-visible, .classroom-root .quantity-note .quantity-check textarea:focus-visible { outline: 2px solid #006d70; outline-offset: 3px; }\\r\\n  .classroom-root .quantity…8609 tokens truncated…ce premise.</td>\\r\\n</tr>\\r\\n<tr>\\r\\n<td><strong>Few</strong>, <strong>not many</strong>, <strong>not often</strong></td>\\r\\n<td>A small number or low frequency in context. No exact numerical boundary follows.</td>\\r\\n</tr>\\r\\n<tr>\\r\\n<td><strong>No</strong>, <strong>none</strong>, <strong>zero</strong></td>\\r\\n<td>Not a single member fits.</td>\\r\\n</tr>\\r\\n<tr>\\r\\n<td><strong>Not all</strong></td>\\r\\n<td>At least one member does <strong>not</strong> fit.</td>\\r\\n</tr>\\r\\n</tbody></table>\\r\\n<p><strong>Few</strong> is worth separating from <strong>a few</strong>. “A few books are hardcover” affirms that at least some are. “Few books are hardcover” stresses how small the number is. It often suggests some in ordinary speech, but it does not set a dependable numerical minimum. “Not many” can include zero. A hundred menu items could make five feel like few; in a menu of six, five would not feel like few. Do not quietly replace any of these phrases with a fixed number.</p>\\r\\n<p><strong>Most</strong> is different: it crosses a precise line, <strong>more than half</strong>. Words such as <strong>the majority</strong> plainly express that idea. Words such as <strong>usually</strong>, <strong>typically</strong>, <strong>generally</strong>, <strong>commonly</strong>, <strong>frequently</strong>, <strong>often</strong>, <strong>likely</strong>, and <strong>probably</strong> can suggest prevalence in context, but pay attention to what the sentence actually counts. “Probably” about one event, for example, is a likelihood claim, not a headcount of a group.</p>\\r\\n<p><strong>Not all</strong> <em>does</em> give us a precise minimum: at least one exception. “Not all club members are lawyers” means <strong>at least one club member is not a lawyer</strong>. It does not tell us whether the other members are lawyers.</p>\\r\\n<aside class=\\"quantity-check\\" data-check=\\"10\\"><div class=\\"quantity-check-top\\"><span>Quick check</span><span>11</span></div><p class=\\"quantity-check-question\\"> A menu lists 100 beers. “Many are Belgian,” “few are Belgian,” and “not many are Belgian” are three claims. Can you assign each one an exact minimum and maximum? What does “not all are Belgian” guarantee?</p><form class=\\"quantity-check-form\\"><label for=\\"quantity-check-10\\">Your answer</label><textarea id=\\"quantity-check-10\\" rows=\\"2\\" placeholder=\\"Write your answer here…\\" required></textarea><button type=\\"submit\\">Check my answer <span aria-hidden=\\"true\\">↗</span></button></form><div class=\\"quantity-check-result\\" hidden aria-live=\\"polite\\"><strong></strong><div class=\\"quantity-check-worked\\"><div class=\\"quantity-answer-body\\"><p>The first three do <strong>not</strong> have fixed numerical boundaries. “Many” suggests more than an isolated example, while “few” and “not many” suggest a small number relative to the context. None alone licenses a specific cutoff such as five or ten. “Not all” is exact about one thing: <strong>at least one beer is not Belgian</strong>.</p>\\r\\n</div></div><button type=\\"button\\" class=\\"quantity-check-again\\">Edit my answer</button></div></aside>\\r\\n\\r\\n</section><section class=\\"quantity-quiz\\" id=\\"quantity-quiz\\" aria-labelledby=\\"quantity-quiz-title\\">\\r\\n  <div class=\\"quantity-quiz-top\\"><span>Practice</span><span class=\\"quantity-quiz-progress\\" id=\\"quantity-quiz-progress\\">01 / 07</span></div>\\r\\n  <div class=\\"quantity-quiz-track\\" aria-hidden=\\"true\\"><span id=\\"quantity-quiz-track-fill\\"></span></div>\\r\\n  <h3 id=\\"quantity-quiz-title\\">Can you prove the overlap?</h3>\\r\\n  <p class=\\"quantity-quiz-intro\\">Seven short answers. Make a call, explain it, then compare your reasoning.</p>\\r\\n  <div class=\\"quantity-quiz-stage\\">\\r\\n    <div class=\\"quantity-quiz-premises\\" id=\\"quantity-quiz-premises\\"><span>Given</span><strong>Some A are B.</strong><strong>All B are C.</strong></div>\\r\\n    <div class=\\"quantity-quiz-bridge\\" id=\\"quantity-quiz-bridge\\" aria-hidden=\\"true\\"><span>A</span><i></i><span>C</span></div>\\r\\n    <form id=\\"quantity-quiz-form\\" class=\\"quantity-quiz-form\\">\\r\\n      <label for=\\"quantity-quiz-input\\">Must some A be C?</label>\\r\\n      <p>Start with <b>Yes</b> or <b>No</b>, then tell me why.</p>\\r\\n      <textarea id=\\"quantity-quiz-input\\" rows=\\"3\\" placeholder=\\"Yes or no, because…\\" required></textarea>\\r\\n      <div class=\\"quantity-quiz-actions\\"><button type=\\"submit\\">Check my answer <span aria-hidden=\\"true\\">↗</span></button><span id=\\"quantity-quiz-error\\" role=\\"alert\\"></span></div>\\r\\n    </form>\\r\\n    <div class=\\"quantity-quiz-feedback\\" id=\\"quantity-quiz-feedback\\" hidden tabindex=\\"-1\\" aria-live=\\"polite\\">\\r\\n      <div class=\\"quantity-quiz-feedback-label\\" id=\\"quantity-quiz-feedback-label\\"></div>\\r\\n      <p class=\\"quantity-quiz-verdict\\" id=\\"quantity-quiz-verdict\\"></p>\\r\\n      <p class=\\"quantity-quiz-explanation\\" id=\\"quantity-quiz-explanation\\"></p>\\r\\n      <p class=\\"quantity-quiz-reflection\\">Compare your explanation with this one. Did you identify the same person or the same gap?</p>\\r\\n      <button class=\\"quantity-quiz-next\\" id=\\"quantity-quiz-next\\" type=\\"button\\">Next question <span aria-hidden=\\"true\\">→</span></button>\\r\\n    </div>\\r\\n  </div>\\r\\n  <div class=\\"quantity-quiz-done\\" id=\\"quantity-quiz-done\\" hidden><span>Practice complete</span><strong id=\\"quantity-quiz-score\\"></strong><p>You checked each conclusion against a witness or a possible gap. Review any answer where your reason differed, even if your yes or no matched.</p><button id=\\"quantity-quiz-restart\\" type=\\"button\\">Try the seven again ↺</button></div>\\r\\n</section>\\r\\n\\r\\n<section class=\\"quantity-drill\\"><h2 id=\\"drill\\" class=\\"quantity-chapter\\"><span class=\\"quantity-chapter-number\\">11</span>Short drill: diagram, combine, decide</h2>\\r\\n<p>Here are five statements about one volunteer program. <strong>Volunteer</strong>, <strong>Spanish speaker</strong>, <strong>trained person</strong>, <strong>weekend worker</strong>, and <strong>student</strong> name groups of people. Treat each sentence as true.</p>\\r\\n<ol>\\r\\n<li>Some volunteers speak Spanish.</li>\\r\\n<li>All Spanish speakers are trained.</li>\\r\\n<li>Most volunteers work weekends.</li>\\r\\n<li>Most volunteers are students.</li>\\r\\n<li>Most weekend workers are trained.</li>\\r\\n</ol>\\r\\n<p class=\\"quantity-drill-diagram-note\\">Diagram the five statements for yourself. Then work through these four questions. Your answer stays on the page while you compare it with the worked reason.</p><section class=\\"quantity-check quantity-drill-check\\" data-drill=\\"0\\"><div class=\\"quantity-check-top\\"><span>Question 1</span><span>01 / 04</span></div><p class=\\"quantity-check-question\\">Which two statements prove that some volunteers are trained?</p><form class=\\"quantity-check-form\\"><label for=\\"quantity-drill-0\\">Your answer</label><textarea id=\\"quantity-drill-0\\" rows=\\"2\\" placeholder=\\"Write your answer here…\\" required></textarea><button type=\\"submit\\">Check my answer <span aria-hidden=\\"true\\">↗</span></button></form><div class=\\"quantity-check-result\\" hidden aria-live=\\"polite\\"><strong></strong><div class=\\"quantity-check-worked\\"><p>Statements 1 and 2. At least one volunteer speaks Spanish. Every Spanish speaker is trained. That volunteer is trained.</p></div><button type=\\"button\\" class=\\"quantity-check-again\\">Edit my answer</button></div></section><section class=\\"quantity-check quantity-drill-check\\" data-drill=\\"1\\"><div class=\\"quantity-check-top\\"><span>Question 2</span><span>02 / 04</span></div><p class=\\"quantity-check-question\\">Which two statements prove that some students work weekends?</p><form class=\\"quantity-check-form\\"><label for=\\"quantity-drill-1\\">Your answer</label><textarea id=\\"quantity-drill-1\\" rows=\\"2\\" placeholder=\\"Write your answer here…\\" required></textarea><button type=\\"submit\\">Check my answer <span aria-hidden=\\"true\\">↗</span></button></form><div class=\\"quantity-check-result\\" hidden aria-live=\\"polite\\"><strong></strong><div class=\\"quantity-check-worked\\"><p>Statements 3 and 4. Both majorities count volunteers, so the students and weekend workers must share at least one volunteer.</p></div><button type=\\"button\\" class=\\"quantity-check-again\\">Edit my answer</button></div></section><section class=\\"quantity-check quantity-drill-check\\" data-drill=\\"2\\"><div class=\\"quantity-check-top\\"><span>Question 3</span><span>03 / 04</span></div><p class=\\"quantity-check-question\\">Do statements 3 and 5 prove that most volunteers are trained? Explain.</p><form class=\\"quantity-check-form\\"><label for=\\"quantity-drill-2\\">Your answer</label><textarea id=\\"quantity-drill-2\\" rows=\\"2\\" placeholder=\\"Write your answer here…\\" required></textarea><button type=\\"submit\\">Check my answer <span aria-hidden=\\"true\\">↗</span></button></form><div class=\\"quantity-check-result\\" hidden aria-live=\\"polite\\"><strong></strong><div class=\\"quantity-check-worked\\"><p>No. Statement 3 counts volunteers; statement 5 counts all weekend workers. The volunteer weekend workers could be in the untrained minority of weekend workers.</p></div><button type=\\"button\\" class=\\"quantity-check-again\\">Edit my answer</button></div></section><section class=\\"quantity-check quantity-drill-check\\" data-drill=\\"3\\"><div class=\\"quantity-check-top\\"><span>Question 4</span><span>04 / 04</span></div><p class=\\"quantity-check-question\\">Can either valid some conclusion be reversed? That one A proves the overlap.\\"},{\\"premises\\":[\\"Some A are B.\\",\\"Most A are C.\\"],\\"answer\\":\\"no\\",\\"explanation\\":\\"The A that is B could be outside the majority of A that is C. Two some claims need not share a witness.\\"},{\\"premises\\":[\\"Most A are B.\\",\\"Most A are C.\\"],\\"answer\\":\\"yes\\",\\"explanation\\":\\"Both majorities count the same A group. Add at least one A, and the overlap follows.\\"}];\\r\\nconst quizForm=document.getElementById(\'quantity-quiz-form\');\\r\\nconst quizInput=document.getElementById(\'quantity-quiz-input\');\\r\\nconst quizFeedback=document.getElementById(\'quantity-quiz-feedback\');\\r\\nconst quizBridge=document.getElementById(\'quantity-quiz-bridge\');\\r\\nlet quizIndex=0,quizMatches=0;\\r\\nfunction renderQuiz(){const q=quizQuestions[quizIndex];document.getElementById(\'quantity-quiz-progress\').textContent=String(quizIndex+1).padStart(2,\'0\')+\' / 07\';document.getElementById(\'quantity-quiz-track-fill\').style.width=((quizIndex+1)/quizQuestions.length*100)+\'%\';document.getElementById(\'quantity-quiz-premises\').replaceChildren(Object.assign(document.createElement(\'span\'),{textContent:\'Given\'}),...q.premises.map(line=>Object.assign(document.createElement(\'strong\'),{textContent:line})));quizInput.value=\'\';quizForm.hidden=false;quizFeedback.hidden=true;quizBridge.classList.remove(\'is-yes\',\'is-no\');document.getElementById(\'quantity-quiz-error\').textContent=\'\';}\\r\\nquizForm.addEventListener(\'submit\',event=>{event.preventDefault();const response=quizInput.value.trim();const choice=/^(yes|yeah|yep|no|not guaranteed|cannot|can\'t|does not|doesn\'t)(?=[^a-z]|$)/i.exec(response);if(!choice){document.getElementById(\'quantity-quiz-error\').textContent=\'Begin with Yes or No, then give your reason.\';quizInput.focus();return}const verdict=/^(yes|yeah|yep)/i.test(choice[1])?\'yes\':\'no\';const q=quizQuestions[quizIndex];const The overlap follows.\':\'No. The overlap is not guaranteed.\';document.getElementById(\'quantity-quiz-explanation\').textContent=q.explanation;document.getElementById(\'quantity-quiz-next\').innerHTML=quizIndex===quizQuestions.length-1?\'See your review <span aria-hidden=\\"true\\">→</span>\':\'Next question <span aria-hidden=\\"true\\">→</span>\';quizBridge.classList.add(q.answer===\'yes\'?\'is-yes\':\'is-no\');quizForm.hidden=true;quizFeedback.hidden=false;quizFeedback.focus();});\\r\\ndocument.getElementById(\'quantity-quiz-next\').addEventListener(\'click\',()=>{if(quizIndex===quizQuestions.length-1){quizStage.hidden=true;quizDone.hidden=false;document.getElementById(\'quantity-quiz-score\').textContent=quizMatches+\' of 7 conclusions matched\';document.getElementById(\'quantity-quiz-progress\').textContent=\'07 / 07\';}else{quizIndex++;renderQuiz();quizInput.focus()}});\\r\\ndocument.getElementById(\'quantity-quiz-restart\').addEventListener(\'click\',()=>{quizIndex=0;quizMatches=0;quizDone.hidden=true;quizStage.hidden=false;renderQuiz();quizInput.focus()});\\r\\n</script>\\r\\n</body></html>\\r\\n"}'}]

margin-right: 0; }\\r\\n  .classroom-root .quantity-note > p { max-width: 620px; }\\r\\n  .classroom-root .quantity-note > figure,\\r\\n  .classroom-root .quantity-note > pre,\\r\\n  .classroom-root .quantity-note > table { max-width: 720px; }\\r\\n  .classroom-root .quantity-note .quantity-language { max-width: 720px; margin-left: auto; margin-right: auto; }\\r\\n  @media (prefers-reduced-motion: reduce) { .quantity-progress, .classroom-root .quantity-note .quantity-lab-book, .classroom-root .quantity-note .quantity-lab-verdicts span, .classroom-root .quantity-note .quantity-person, .classroom-root .quantity-note .quantity-quiz * { transition: none !important; animation: none !important; } .classroom-root .quantity-note .quantity-lab-book.is-new::after { display: none; } }\\r\\n</style>\\r\\n</head><body>\\r\\n<div class=\\"quantity-progress\\" aria-hidden=\\"true\\"></div>\\r\\n<div class=\\"classroom-root\\">\\r\\n  <aside class=\\"classroom-sidebar\\"><div class=\\"classroom-sidebar-card\\"><div class=\\"classroom-panel-inner\\">\\r\\n    <div class=\\"classroom-brand\\"><i>U</i><span>LSAT U</span></div>\\r\\n    <span class=\\"preview-rail-label\\">Classroom</span>\\r\\n    <div class=\\"classroom-nav-group\\">\\r\\n      <div class=\\"classroom-nav\\">Home</div>\\r\\n      <div class=\\"classroom-nav classroom-nav-active\\">Notes and recaps</div>\\r\\n      <div class=\\"classroom-nav\\">Assigned work</div>\\r\\n      <div class=\\"classroom-nav\\">Flagged</div>\\r\\n      <div class=\\"classroom-nav\\">Wrong answers</div>\\r\\n    </div>\\r\\n  </div></div></aside>\\r\\n  <main class=\\"classroom-stage\\"><div class=\\"classroom-column\\">\\r\\n    <div class=\\"classroom-dateline\\"><time>September 29, 2026</time><span>|</span><span>Note</span></div>\\r\\n    <h1 class=\\"classroom-title\\">Some, Most, and All</h1>\\r\\n    <article class=\\"classroom-prose quantity-note\\"><header class=\\"quantity-opening\\">\\r\\n  <div class=\\"quantity-opening-story\\">\\r\\n  <p class=\\"quantity-lead\\">Quantity statements tell us whether a claim applies to some members of a group, most of them, or all of them.</p>\\r\\n  <p class=\\"quantity-scenario\\">Suppose a class has been assigned a case to read. These are three different claims about that same class:</p>\\r\\n  <div class=\\"quantity-claims\\" aria-label=\\"Three claims about the same class\\">\\r\\n    <div class=\\"quantity-claim\\"><b>Some</b><span>students read it.</span></div>\\r\\n    <div class=\\"quantity-claim\\"><b>Most</b><span>students read it.</span></div>\\r\\n    <div class=\\"quantity-claim\\"><b>All</b><span>students read it.</span></div>\\r\\n  </div>\\r\\n  <p class=\\"quantity-scenario\\">We do not need an exact headcount to understand the difference.</p>\\r\\n  <div class=\\"quantity-opening-question\\"><p>What must be true?</p><p>What could still be true?</p><small>The answer changes with the word we use.</small></div>\\r\\n  </div>\\r\\n  <div class=\\"quantity-lab\\" aria-label=\\"Explore quantity statements with eight books\\">\\r\\n    <div class=\\"quantity-lab-head\\"><span class=\\"quantity-lab-kicker\\">A little experiment</span><span class=\\"quantity-lab-count\\" aria-live=\\"polite\\"><b id=\\"quantity-count\\">1</b> of 8 books are hardcover</span></div>\\r\\n    <div class=\\"quantity-lab-books\\" aria-hidden=\\"true\\"><span class=\\"quantity-lab-book is-hardcover\\"><i></i></span><span class=\\"quantity-lab-book\\"><i></i></span><span class=\\"quantity-lab-book\\"><i></i></span><span class=\\"quantity-lab-book\\"><i></i></span><span class=\\"quantity-lab-book\\"><i></i></span><span class=\\"quantity-lab-book\\"><i></i></span><span class=\\"quantity-lab-book\\"><i></i></span><span class=\\"quantity-lab-book\\"><i></i></span></div>\\r\\n    <label class=\\"quantity-lab-instruction\\" for=\\"quantity-slider\\">Move the count. Watch which statements become true.</label>\\r\\n    <input id=\\"quantity-slider\\" class=\\"quantity-lab-slider\\" type=\\"range\\" min=\\"0\\" max=\\"8\\" value=\\"1\\" aria-label=\\"Number of hardcover books\\">\\r\\n    <div class=\\"quantity-lab-verdicts\\" aria-live=\\"polite\\"><span data-rule=\\"some\\" class=\\"is-true\\"><i></i><b>Some</b><small>1–8</small></span><span data-rule=\\"most\\"><i></i><b>Most</b><small>5–8</small></span><span data-rule=\\"all\\"><i></i><b>All</b><small>8</small></span></div>\\r\\n  </div>\\r\\n</header>\\r\\n<nav class=\\"quantity-jump\\" aria-label=\\"Jump through the lesson\\"><a href=\\"#some\\">Some</a><a href=\\"#most\\">Most</a><a href=\\"#all\\">All</a><a href=\\"#inferences\\">Inferences</a><a href=\\"#quantity-quiz\\">Practice</a><a href=\\"#drill\\">Drill</a></nav><h2 id=\\"some\\" class=\\"quantity-chapter\\"><span class=\\"quantity-chapter-number\\">01</span>Some guarantees at least one</h2>\\r\\n<p>Imagine a shelf with eight books. I tell you that <strong>some of the books are hardcover</strong>. How many hardcover books must there be?</p>\\r\\n<p>At least <strong>one</strong>. There could be one, four, or all eight. “Some” rules out zero. It does not rule out “most” or “all.”</p>\\r\\n<figure class=\\"quantity-figure\\"><img src=\\"./assets/some-books.svg\\" alt=\\"Eight books showing that one hardcover book and eight hardcover books both satisfy some.\\"></figure>\\r\\n<p>This is easy to miss because in ordinary conversation “some” often suggests “some, but not all.” On the LSAT, that extra phrase is not part of the statement. If all eight books are hardcover, it is still true that some are hardcover.</p>\\r\\n<aside class=\\"quantity-check\\" data-check=\\"0\\"><div class=\\"quantity-check-top\\"><span>Try it</span><span>01</span></div><p class=\\"quantity-check-question\\"> “Some applicants received an interview.” Could every applicant have received one? Does the sentence tell us that anyone was denied an interview?</p><form class=\\"quantity-check-form\\"><label for=\\"quantity-check-0\\">Your answer</label><textarea id=\\"quantity-check-0\\" rows=\\"2\\" placeholder=\\"Write your answer here…\\" required></textarea><button type=\\"submit\\">Check my answer <span aria-hidden=\\"true\\">↗</span></button></form><div class=\\"quantity-check-result\\" hidden aria-live=\\"polite\\"><strong></strong><div class=\\"quantity-check-worked\\"><div class=\\"quantity-answer-body\\"><p>Every applicant could have received an interview. The sentence does not tell us that anyone was denied one. “Some” guarantees at least one applicant received an interview, but it leaves open how many others did.</p>\\r\\n</div></div><button type=\\"button\\" class=\\"quantity-check-again\\">Edit my answer</button></div></aside>\\r\\n\\r\\n<p>Whenever you read <strong>some</strong>, replace it with <strong>at least one</strong> in your head. That tells you exactly what the sentence guarantees:</p>\\r\\n<blockquote>\\r\\n<p><strong>Some of the books are hardcover.</strong></p>\\r\\n<p><strong>At least one of the books is hardcover.</strong></p>\\r\\n</blockquote>\\r\\n<p>Now focus on that one book. It belongs to both groups: it is a book, and it is hardcover. If we describe the same object from the hardcover side, we can say:</p>\\r\\n<blockquote>\\r\\n<p><strong>Some hardcover items are books.</strong></p>\\r\\n<p><strong>At least one hardcover item is a book.</strong></p>\\r\\n</blockquote>\\r\\n<p>We have not found a new object. We have described the same overlap in the other direction. That is why a <strong>some</strong> statement works both ways:</p>\\r\\n<pre><code class=\\"language-text\\">Books  ←some→  Hardcover\\r\\n</code></pre>\\r\\n<p>The double arrow tells us that at least one thing belongs to both groups. It does not tell us that every book is hardcover or that every hardcover item is a book.</p>\\r\\n<h3>What would make “some” false?</h3>\\r\\n<p>If <strong>some books are hardcover</strong> is false, then <strong>no books are hardcover</strong>. There is not even one book in both groups. Notice the difference between <strong>no books are hardcover</strong> and <strong>some books are not hardcover</strong>. The second sentence still allows other books to be hardcover, so it does not negate “some.”</p>\\r\\n<h2 id=\\"most\\" class=\\"quantity-chapter\\"><span class=\\"quantity-chapter-number\\">02</span>Most guarantees more than half</h2>\\r\\n<p>Now I tell you that <strong>most of the eight books are hardcover</strong>. We need at least <strong>five</strong> hardcover books. Four would be exactly half, and half is not most. Five, six, seven, or eight could satisfy the sentence.</p>\\r\\n<figure class=\\"quantity-figure\\"><img src=\\"./assets/most-books.svg\\" alt=\\"Eight books showing that five hardcover books and eight hardcover books both satisfy most.\\"></figure>\\r\\n<p>Notice that all eight could still be hardcover. “Most” does not tell us that an exception exists.</p>\\r\\n<aside class=\\"quantity-check\\" data-check=\\"1\\"><div class=\\"quantity-check-top\\"><span>Try it</span><span>02</span></div><p class=\\"quantity-check-question\\"> If most of the eight books are hardcover, must some of them be hardcover? Must any be paperback?</p><form class=\\"quantity-check-form\\"><label for=\\"quantity-check-1\\">Your answer</label><textarea id=\\"quantity-check-1\\" rows=\\"2\\" placeholder=\\"Write your answer here…\\" required></textarea><button type=\\"submit\\">Check my answer <span aria-hidden=\\"true\\">↗</span></button></form><div class=\\"quantity-check-result\\" hidden aria-live=\\"polite\\"><strong></strong><div class=\\"quantity-check-worked\\"><div class=\\"quantity-answer-body\\"><p>At least five are hardcover, so <strong>some are hardcover</strong>. No paperback book is required. All eight could be hardcover.</p>\\r\\n</div></div><button type=\\"button\\" class=\\"quantity-check-again\\">Edit my answer</button></div></aside>\\r\\n\\r\\n<p>Here is where the direction of the statement matters. From “most books are hardcover,” we know that <strong>some hardcover things are books</strong>. We cannot say that <strong>most hardcover things are books</strong>. Five of our eight shelf books could be hardcover, while a library holds hundreds of other hardcover items. The first statement counts the eight books; the reversed statement counts a different group.</p>\\r\\n<pre><code class=\\"language-text\\">Books  ─most→  Hardcover\\r\\nHardcover  ←some→  Books\\r\\n</code></pre>\\r\\n<p>The first arrow gives the majority claim. The second line is the overlap that the majority guarantees. When we read a most statement backward, the word becomes <strong>some</strong>.</p>\\r\\n<aside class=\\"quantity-check\\" data-check=\\"2\\"><div class=\\"quantity-check-top\\"><span>Quick check</span><span>03</span></div><p class=\\"quantity-check-question\\"> A program has 100 applicants. Most speak Spanish. What is the smallest possible number who speak Spanish? What is the largest?</p><form class=\\"quantity-check-form\\"><label for=\\"quantity-check-2\\">Your answer</label><textarea id=\\"quantity-check-2\\" rows=\\"2\\" placeholder=\\"Write your answer here…\\" required></textarea><button type=\\"submit\\">Check my answer <span aria-hidden=\\"true\\">↗</span></button></form><div class=\\"quantity-check-result\\" hidden aria-live=\\"polite\\"><strong></strong><div class=\\"quantity-check-worked\\"><div class=\\"quantity-answer-body\\"><p><strong>51 to 100.</strong> Fifty is only half. “Most” also permits all 100, so it does not establish an exception.</p>\\r\\n</div></div><button type=\\"button\\" class=\\"quantity-check-again\\">Edit my answer</button></div></aside>\\r\\n\\r\\n<h3>What would make “most” false?</h3>\\r\\n<p>“Most of the eight books are hardcover” needs at least five hardcover books. Its negation permits <strong>four or fewer</strong>. Four is exactly half, so it already makes “most” false. The opposite of “most are hardcover” is <strong>half or fewer are hardcover</strong>, not “most are paperback.” Both kinds could make up exactly half.</p>\\r\\n<h2 id=\\"all\\" class=\\"quantity-chapter\\"><span class=\\"quantity-chapter-number\\">03</span>All leaves no exception</h2>\\r\\n<p>If <strong>all eight books are hardcover</strong>, every book on the shelf is hardcover. One paperback on that shelf would make the statement false.</p>\\r\\n<figure class=\\"quantity-figure\\"><img src=\\"./assets/all-books.svg\\" alt=\\"Eight books showing that all eight must be hardcover to satisfy all.\\"></figure>\\r\\n<p>An all statement gives us a rule: <strong>if it is one of these eight books, then it is hardcover</strong>.</p>\\r\\n<pre><code class=\\"language-text\\">One of these books  →  Hardcover\\r\\nNot hardcover       →  Not one of these books\\r\\n</code></pre>\\r\\n<p>The second line is the contrapositive. Both lines go with the same rule. We cannot turn the first line around and conclude that every hardcover thing is a book on this shelf.</p>\\r\\n<aside class=\\"quantity-check\\" data-check=\\"3\\"><div class=\\"quantity-check-top\\"><span>Try it</span><span>04</span></div><p class=\\"quantity-check-question\\"> You pick up a paperback. What does the rule tell you? What if you pick up a hardcover book from another shelf?</p><form class=\\"quantity-check-form\\"><label for=\\"quantity-check-3\\">Your answer</label><textarea id=\\"quantity-check-3\\" rows=\\"2\\" placeholder=\\"Write your answer here…\\" required></textarea><button type=\\"submit\\">Check my answer <span aria-hidden=\\"true\\">↗</span></button></form><div class=\\"quantity-check-result\\" hidden aria-live=\\"polite\\"><strong></strong><div class=\\"quantity-check-worked\\"><div class=\\"quantity-answer-body\\"><p>The paperback cannot be one of these eight books. A hardcover book from another shelf does not become one of these eight just because it is hardcover. Being hardcover is required, but it is not enough to identify one of these books.</p>\\r\\n</div></div><button type=\\"button\\" class=\\"quantity-check-again\\">Edit my answer</button></div></aside>\\r\\n\\r\\n<p>There is a small qualification. “All A are B” gives a rule about A. By itself, it does not tell us that any A exist. When we already know the group exists, all also guarantees most and some.</p>\\r\\n<h3>What would make “all” false?</h3>\\r\\n<p>One exception is enough. “Not all eight books are hardcover” means <strong>at least one of the eight is not hardcover</strong>. The other seven could still be hardcover. This is the negation of the all claim. By contrast, “not hardcover → not one of these eight books” is its <strong>contrapositive</strong>: another way to state the original rule, not a way to deny it.</p>\\r\\n<h2 class=\\"quantity-chapter\\"><span class=\\"quantity-chapter-number\\">04</span>Put the three statements side by side</h2>\\r\\n<p>The same shelf can help us see the whole range.</p>\\r\\n<figure class=\\"quantity-figure\\"><img src=\\"./assets/book-quantity-ranges.svg\\" alt=\\"For eight existing books: some permits one through eight hardcover, most permits five through eight, and all requires eight.\\"></figure>\\r\\n<p>Read the implications in one direction:</p>\\r\\n<pre><code class=\\"language-text\\">ALL  →  MOST  →  SOME       (when the group exists)\\r\\n</code></pre>\\r\\n<p>If all eight books are hardcover, then most are, and some are. If only “some” is given, all eight <strong>could</strong> be hardcover, but we cannot conclude that they <strong>are</strong>. Keep the distinction between a possible situation and a required one.</p>\\r\\n<h2 id=\\"inferences\\" class=\\"quantity-chapter\\"><span class=\\"quantity-chapter-number\\">05</span>Carry a claim through an all rule</h2>\\r\\n<p>Now we can combine statements. Suppose <strong>some library books are mysteries</strong> and <strong>all mysteries are fiction</strong>. We have at least one book that is a mystery. The all rule applies to every mystery, including that one. So <strong>some library books are fiction</strong>.</p>\\r\\n<pre><code class=\\"language-text\\">Library books  ←some→  Mysteries  →  Fiction\\r\\nLibrary books  ←some→  Fiction\\r\\n</code></pre>\\r\\n<p>The all arrow points <strong>away from</strong> the mystery group we reached. That is why the chain works. If the second sentence said only “most mysteries are fiction,” the mystery books we found could be among the exceptions.</p>\\r\\n<figure class=\\"quantity-figure\\"><img src=\\"./assets/some-all-witness.svg\\" alt=\\"A marked library book is inside the mysteries group, which is entirely inside the fiction group.\\"></figure>\\r\\n<aside class=\\"quantity-check\\" data-check=\\"4\\"><div class=\\"quantity-check-top\\"><span>Quick check</span><span>05</span></div><p class=\\"quantity-check-question\\"> Some archive documents are letters. All letters are handwritten. What follows?</p><form class=\\"quantity-check-form\\"><label for=\\"quantity-check-4\\">Your answer</label><textarea id=\\"quantity-check-4\\" rows=\\"2\\" placeholder=\\"Write your answer here…\\" required></textarea><button type=\\"submit\\">Check my answer <span aria-hidden=\\"true\\">↗</span></button></form><div class=\\"quantity-check-result\\" hidden aria-live=\\"polite\\"><strong></strong><div class=\\"quantity-check-worked\\"><div class=\\"quantity-answer-body\\"><p><strong>Some archive documents are handwritten.</strong> Take a document guaranteed to be a letter. The all rule applies to that document.</p>\\r\\n</div></div><button type=\\"button\\" class=\\"quantity-check-again\\">Edit my answer</button></div></aside>\\r\\n\\r\\n<p>The same reasoning preserves the first quantity word:</p>\\r\\n<table>\\r\\n<thead>\\r\\n<tr>\\r\\n<th>First statement</th>\\r\\n<th>Rule</th>\\r\\n<th>What follows</th>\\r\\n</tr>\\r\\n</thead>\\r\\n<tbody><tr>\\r\\n<td>Some library books are mysteries.</td>\\r\\n<td>All mysteries are fiction.</td>\\r\\n<td>Some library books are fiction.</td>\\r\\n</tr>\\r\\n<tr>\\r\\n<td>Most library books are mysteries.</td>\\r\\n<td>All mysteries are fiction.</td>\\r\\n<td>Most library books are fiction.</td>\\r\\n</tr>\\r\\n<tr>\\r\\n<td>All library books are mysteries.</td>\\r\\n<td>All mysteries are fiction.</td>\\r\\n<td>All library books are fiction.</td>\\r\\n</tr>\\r\\n</tbody></table>\\r\\n<aside class=\\"quantity-check\\" data-check=\\"5\\"><div class=\\"quantity-check-top\\"><span>Try it</span><span>06</span></div><p class=\\"quantity-check-question\\"> “Most library books are mysteries. All fiction is mystery.” Can we conclude that most library books are fiction?</p><form class=\\"quantity-check-form\\"><label for=\\"quantity-check-5\\">Your answer</label><textarea id=\\"quantity-check-5\\" rows=\\"2\\" placeholder=\\"Write your answer here…\\" required></textarea><button type=\\"submit\\">Check my answer <span aria-hidden=\\"true\\">↗</span></button></form><div class=\\"quantity-check-result\\" hidden aria-live=\\"polite\\"><strong></strong><div class=\\"quantity-check-worked\\"><div class=\\"quantity-answer-body\\"><p>No. The all arrow goes from fiction to mystery. We cannot start at mystery and travel backward to fiction.</p>\\r\\n</div></div><button type=\\"button\\" class=\\"quantity-check-again\\">Edit my answer</button></div></aside>\\r\\n\\r\\n<h2 class=\\"quantity-chapter\\"><span class=\\"quantity-chapter-number\\">06</span>Two majorities of the same group must meet</h2>\\r\\n<p>Suppose a club has ten members. <strong>Most members study law</strong>, and <strong>most members work part time</strong>. Each “most” needs at least six of the ten members. Those two sets cannot fit inside the club without sharing at least two people.</p>\\r\\n<section class=\\"quantity-overlap-lab\\" aria-label=\\"Explore why two majorities overlap\\">\\r\\n    <div class=\\"quantity-overlap-head\\"><h3>Try to separate the majorities.</h3><p>Six of ten club members study law. Six work part time. Rearrange the workers and watch what cannot change.</p></div>\\r\\n    <div class=\\"quantity-overlap-people\\" id=\\"quantity-overlap-people\\" aria-label=\\"Ten club members\\"><span class=\\"quantity-person studies-law \\" role=\\"img\\" aria-label=\\"Member 1: studies law, does not work part time\\">01</span><span class=\\"quantity-person studies-law \\" role=\\"img\\" aria-label=\\"Member 2: studies law, does not work part time\\">02</span><span class=\\"quantity-person studies-law \\" role=\\"img\\" aria-label=\\"Member 3: studies law, does not work part time\\">03</span><span class=\\"quantity-person studies-law \\" role=\\"img\\" aria-label=\\"Member 4: studies law, does not work part time\\">04</span><span class=\\"quantity-person studies-law works-part-time\\" role=\\"img\\" aria-label=\\"Member 5: studies law, works part time\\">05</span><span class=\\"quantity-person studies-law works-part-time\\" role=\\"img\\" aria-label=\\"Member 6: studies law, works part time\\">06</span><span class=\\"quantity-person works-part-time\\" role=\\"img\\" aria-label=\\"Member 7: does not study law, works part time\\">07</span><span class=\\"quantity-person works-part-time\\" role=\\"img\\" aria-label=\\"Member 8: does not study law, works part time\\">08</span><span class=\\"quantity-person works-part-time\\" role=\\"img\\" aria-label=\\"Member 9: does not study law, works part time\\">09</span><span class=\\"quantity-person works-part-time\\" role=\\"img\\" aria-label=\\"Member 10: does not study law, works part time\\">10</span></div>\\r\\n    <div class=\\"quantity-overlap-legend\\"><span><i class=\\"law\\"></i>Studies law</span><span><i class=\\"work\\"></i>Works part time</span><span><i class=\\"both\\"></i>Both</span></div>\\r\\n    <div class=\\"quantity-overlap-footer\\"><p aria-live=\\"polite\\"><b id=\\"quantity-overlap-count\\">2</b> members have both traits. <span id=\\"quantity-overlap-message\\">Even the smallest possible overlap is not zero.</span></p><button id=\\"quantity-overlap-shuffle\\" type=\\"button\\">Rearrange the workers <span aria-hidden=\\"true\\">↻</span></button></div>\\r\\n    <div class=\\"quantity-venn\\"><p><b>The universe is the ten club members.</b> The shared middle cannot be empty.</p><svg viewBox=\\"0 0 640 278\\" role=\\"img\\" aria-label=\\"Venn diagram of ten club members, updated as the members are rearranged\\"><rect x=\\"8\\" y=\\"8\\" width=\\"624\\" height=\\"262\\" rx=\\"18\\" fill=\\"#fffdfa\\" stroke=\\"#bed6d3\\" stroke-width=\\"2\\"/><text x=\\"30\\" y=\\"37\\" fill=\\"#173047\\" font-size=\\"16\\" font-family=\\"Outfit, sans-serif\\">All ten club members</text><circle cx=\\"259\\" cy=\\"151\\" r=\\"98\\" fill=\\"#48b0a9\\" fill-opacity=\\".36\\" stroke=\\"#00847f\\" stroke-width=\\"2\\"/><circle cx=\\"381\\" cy=\\"151\\" r=\\"98\\" fill=\\"#bb9bdf\\" fill-opacity=\\".36\\" stroke=\\"#7650a3\\" stroke-width=\\"2\\"/><text x=\\"195\\" y=\\"98\\" fill=\\"#173047\\" font-size=\\"14\\" font-family=\\"Outfit, sans-serif\\">Study law</text><text x=\\"380\\" y=\\"98\\" fill=\\"#173047\\" font-size=\\"14\\" font-family=\\"Outfit, sans-serif\\">Work part time</text><text id=\\"venn-law-only\\" x=\\"206\\" y=\\"165\\" fill=\\"#173047\\" font-size=\\"27\\" font-weight=\\"700\\" font-family=\\"Outfit, sans-serif\\">4</text><text id=\\"venn-both\\" x=\\"312\\" y=\\"165\\" fill=\\"#173047\\" font-size=\\"27\\" font-weight=\\"700\\" font-family=\\"Outfit, sans-serif\\">2</text><text id=\\"venn-work-only\\" x=\\"415\\" y=\\"165\\" fill=\\"#173047\\" font-size=\\"27\\" font-weight=\\"700\\" font-family=\\"Outfit, sans-serif\\">4</text><text x=\\"30\\" y=\\"247\\" fill=\\"#173047\\" font-size=\\"13\\" font-family=\\"Outfit, sans-serif\\">Neither: <tspan id=\\"venn-neither\\">0</tspan></text></svg><small>The circle areas are schematic. The numbers count members and change when you rearrange them.</small></div>\\r\\n  </section>\\r\\n<p><strong>What is given?</strong> More than half of the <em>same ten members</em> study law. More than half of those same ten work part time. The claims count club members, not all law students or all part-time workers everywhere.</p>\\r\\n<p><strong>Why must they meet?</strong> Each group needs at least six members. If the groups were separate, we would need twelve different club members. There are only ten. At least two members must be in both groups.</p>\\r\\n<p><strong>What follows?</strong> <strong>Some law students work part time.</strong> We can reverse that some claim: <strong>some part-time workers study law</strong>. Both sentences describe the same overlapping members. We know at least one exists; we do not know that most law students or most part-time workers have the other trait.</p>\\r\\n<pre><code class=\\"language-text\\">Given:       Most club members study law.\\r\\n             Most club members work part time.\\r\\nMust follow: Some law students work part time.\\r\\nWhy:         Both majorities count the same club members.\\r\\n</code></pre>\\r\\n<aside class=\\"quantity-check\\" data-check=\\"6\\"><div class=\\"quantity-check-top\\"><span>Quick check</span><span>07</span></div><p class=\\"quantity-check-question\\"> A club has 25 members. Most are lawyers, and most play an instrument. What is the smallest possible number who are both lawyers and play an instrument?</p><form class=\\"quantity-check-form\\"><label for=\\"quantity-check-6\\">Your answer</label><textarea id=\\"quantity-check-6\\" rows=\\"2\\" placeholder=\\"Write your answer here…\\" required></textarea><button type=\\"submit\\">Check my answer <span aria-hidden=\\"true\\">↗</span></button></form><div class=\\"quantity-check-result\\" hidden aria-live=\\"polite\\"><strong></strong><div class=\\"quantity-check-worked\\"><div class=\\"quantity-answer-body\\"><p><strong>One.</strong> Each majority needs at least 13 members. If none overlapped, those groups would require 26 different people, but the club has only 25. All 25 could also have both traits.</p>\\r\\n</div></div><button type=\\"button\\" class=\\"quantity-check-again\\">Edit my answer</button></div></aside>\\r\\n\\r\\n<aside class=\\"quantity-check\\" data-check=\\"7\\"><div class=\\"quantity-check-top\\"><span>Try it</span><span>08</span></div><p class=\\"quantity-check-question\\"> If most club members study law and most club members work part time, could the two groups of members be completely separate?</p><form class=\\"quantity-check-form\\"><label for=\\"quantity-check-7\\">Your answer</label><textarea id=\\"quantity-check-7\\" rows=\\"2\\" placeholder=\\"Write your answer here…\\" required></textarea><button type=\\"submit\\">Check my answer <span aria-hidden=\\"true\\">↗</span></button></form><div class=\\"quantity-check-result\\" hidden aria-live=\\"polite\\"><strong></strong><div class=\\"quantity-check-worked\\"><div class=\\"quantity-answer-body\\"><p>No. Two separate majorities would require more members than the club has. At least one member must have both traits.</p>\\r\\n</div></div><button type=\\"button\\" class=\\"quantity-check-again\\">Edit my answer</button></div></aside>\\r\\n\\r\\n<h3>When all describes that same group</h3>\\r\\n<p>There is another way to guarantee an overlap. Suppose <strong>most club members study law</strong>, and <strong>all club members work part time</strong>. We already know there are law students in the club. The all rule tells us that <em>each one of those members</em> also works part time. So <strong>some law students work part time</strong>.</p>\\r\\n<pre><code class=\\"language-text\\">Given:       Most club members study law.\\r\\n             All club members work part time.\\r\\nMust follow: Some law students work part time.\\r\\nWhy:         The law students already found are club members,\\r\\n             so the all rule applies to each of them.\\r\\n</code></pre>\\r\\n<p>We can make the same move with <strong>some</strong>. If <strong>some club members study law</strong> and <strong>all club members work part time</strong>, take the member guaranteed by “some.” That person has both traits. Again, <strong>some law students work part time</strong>. The sentences can be stated in the other order: <strong>all club members work part time; some club members study law</strong>. The same person still proves the overlap.</p>\\r\\n<figure class=\\"quantity-figure\\"><img src=\\"./assets/all-and-overlap.svg\\" alt=\\"An all rule covers every club member. A highlighted law student is therefore also a part-time worker.\\"></figure>\\r\\n<aside class=\\"quantity-check\\" data-check=\\"8\\"><div class=\\"quantity-check-top\\"><span>Try it</span><span>09</span></div><p class=\\"quantity-check-question\\"> All A are B. All A are C. Must some B be C?</p><form class=\\"quantity-check-form\\"><label for=\\"quantity-check-8\\">Your answer</label><textarea id=\\"quantity-check-8\\" rows=\\"2\\" placeholder=\\"Write your answer here…\\" required></textarea><button type=\\"submit\\">Check my answer <span aria-hidden=\\"true\\">↗</span></button></form><div class=\\"quantity-check-result\\" hidden aria-live=\\"polite\\"><strong></strong><div class=\\"quantity-check-worked\\"><div class=\\"quantity-answer-body\\"><p>Not yet. The two all rules tell us what is true <strong>if</strong> something is A, but neither tells us that any A exists. Add “at least one A exists,” and the overlap follows.</p>\\r\\n</div></div><button type=\\"button\\" class=\\"quantity-check-again\\">Edit my answer</button></div></aside>\\r\\n\\r\\n<h2 class=\\"quantity-chapter\\"><span class=\\"quantity-chapter-number\\">07</span>Two majorities can also compare group sizes</h2>\\r\\n<p>Here is a different comparison. <strong>Most debate team members are seniors</strong>, but <strong>most seniors are not on the debate team</strong>. The seniors on the team are more than half of the team and less than half of the seniors. The same overlap takes up a larger share of the team than of the senior class. Therefore, <strong>there are more seniors than debate team members</strong>.</p>\\r\\n<figure class=\\"quantity-figure\\"><img src=\\"./assets/group-size-comparison.svg\\" alt=\\"An example in which six of ten debate team members are seniors, while six of twenty seniors are on the team.\\"></figure>\\r\\n<p>The numbers in the picture are one possible case. The conclusion follows from the two majority claims, not from those particular numbers.</p>\\r\\n<h2 class=\\"quantity-chapter\\"><span class=\\"quantity-chapter-number\\">08</span>Similar chains can leave a gap</h2>\\r\\n<p>Two some statements need not describe the same person or thing:</p>\\r\\n<pre><code class=\\"language-text\\">Some volunteers speak Spanish.\\r\\nSome Spanish speakers are trained.\\r\\n</code></pre>\\r\\n<p>The trained Spanish speakers could all be people who do not volunteer. We cannot conclude that any volunteers are trained.</p>\\r\\n<figure class=\\"quantity-figure\\"><img src=\\"./assets/some-some-gap.svg\\" alt=\\"Two different Spanish speakers satisfy the two some claims without any volunteer being trained.\\"></figure>\\r\\n<p>Replacing the second “some” with “most” does not fix the gap. The Spanish-speaking volunteers could sit in the untrained minority. Even two most statements that count different groups do not automatically produce a new majority claim.</p>\\r\\n<p>Before drawing a conclusion, ask: <strong>Am I carrying a known group through an all rule? Or do two majorities count the same group?</strong> If neither is true, check whether the groups could miss each other.</p>\\r\\n<aside class=\\"quantity-check\\" data-check=\\"9\\"><div class=\\"quantity-check-top\\"><span>Quick check</span><span>10</span></div><p class=\\"quantity-check-question\\"> A club has 13 members. Most are women, and some are dancers. How many female dancers are guaranteed? How many are possible?</p><form class=\\"quantity-check-form\\"><label for=\\"quantity-check-9\\">Your answer</label><textarea id=\\"quantity-check-9\\" rows=\\"2\\" placeholder=\\"Write your answer here…\\" required></textarea><button type=\\"submit\\">Check my answer <span aria-hidden=\\"true\\">↗</span></button></form><div class=\\"quantity-check-result\\" hidden aria-live=\\"polite\\"><strong></strong><div class=\\"quantity-check-worked\\"><div class=\\"quantity-answer-body\\"><p><strong>Zero are guaranteed; as many as 13 are possible.</strong> Seven women and one dancer meet the statements even if that dancer is one of the six men. Both traits could also belong to every member. A most claim and a some claim about the same group do not force the two traits to meet.</p>\\r\\n</div></div><button type=\\"button\\" class=\\"quantity-check-again\\">Edit my answer</button></div></aside>\\r\\n\\r\\n<section class=\\"quantity-summary\\"><h2 class=\\"quantity-chapter\\"><span class=\\"quantity-chapter-number\\">09</span>The moves to keep</h2>\\r\\n<table>\\r\\n<thead>\\r\\n<tr>\\r\\n<th>What you have</th>\\r\\n<th>What you may conclude</th>\\r\\n</tr>\\r\\n</thead>\\r\\n<tbody><tr>\\r\\n<td>Some A are B</td>\\r\\n<td>Some B are A</td>\\r\\n</tr>\\r\\n<tr>\\r\\n<td>Most A are B</td>\\r\\n<td>Some A are B, and some B are A</td>\\r\\n</tr>\\r\\n<tr>\\r\\n<td>All A are B</td>\\r\\n<td>A → B, and not B → not A</td>\\r\\n</tr>\\r\\n<tr>\\r\\n<td>Some/most/all A are B; all B are C</td>\\r\\n<td>The same first quantity of A are C</td>\\r\\n</tr>\\r\\n<tr>\\r\\n"}'}]

For example, statement 3\'s “some B are C” is already given.\\r\\n\\r\\n</details>\\r\\n\\r\\n## Short drill: diagram, combine, decide\\r\\n\\r\\nHere are five statements about one volunteer program. **Volunteer**, **Spanish speaker**, **trained person**, **weekend worker**, and **student** name groups of people. Treat each sentence as true.\\r\\n\\r\\n1. Some volunteers speak Spanish.\\r\\n2. All Spanish speakers are trained.\\r\\n3. Most volunteers work weekends.\\r\\n4. Most volunteers are students.\\r\\n5. Most weekend workers are trained.\\r\\n\\r\\nFirst, diagram each statement. Then answer these without looking below:\\r\\n\\r\\n1. Which two statements prove that **some volunteers are trained**?\\r\\n2. Which two statements prove that **some students work weekends**?\\r\\n3. Do statements 3 and 5 prove that **most volunteers are trained**? Explain the gap or draw a counterexample.\\r\\n4. Can you write either valid **some** conclusion in the reverse order?\\r\\n\\r\\n<details><summary>Show the diagrams and worked answers</summary>\\r\\n\\r\\n```text\\r\\n1. Volunteers       ←some→  Spanish speakers\\r\\n2. Spanish speakers →       Trained people\\r\\n3. Volunteers       ─most→  Weekend workers\\r\\n4. Volunteers       ─most→  Students\\r\\n5. Weekend workers  ─most→  Trained people\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\n**1. Statements 1 and 2.** At least one volunteer speaks Spanish. Every Spanish speaker is trained. That volunteer is trained, so **some volunteers are trained**.\\r\\n\\r\\n**2. Statements 3 and 4.** Both majorities count the volunteers. The weekend workers and students must share at least one volunteer. So **some students work weekends**.\\r\\n\\r\\n**3. No.** Statement 3 says most volunteers work weekends. Statement 5 counts all weekend workers, a different group. The volunteers who work weekends could be among the untrained weekend workers. Nothing requires most volunteers to be trained.\\r\\n\\r\\n**4. Yes.** A some statement describes an overlap in either direction: **some trained people are volunteers**, and **some weekend workers are students**.\\r\\n\\r\\n</details>\\r\\n"}'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '{"chunk_id":"17bac3","wall_time_seconds":1.5538639,"exit_code":0,"original_token_count":1233,"output":"\\"\\"\\"Build the review-only recap upgrade from its two Markdown teaching sources.\\"\\"\\"\\r\\nfrom pathlib import Path\\r\\nimport ast\\r\\nimport html\\r\\nimport re\\r\\n\\r\\n\\r\\nHERE = Path(__file__).resolve().parent\\r\\nROOT = HERE.parents[1]\\r\\nRECAP = ROOT / \\"students/yahunatan-zetvy/recaps/2026-09-16-recap.md\\"\\r\\nUPGRADE = HERE / \\"integration-upgrade.md\\"\\r\\nOUTPUT = HERE / \\"conditional-reasoning-v2.html\\"\\r\\n\\r\\n\\r\\ndef original_literals():\\r\\n    \\"\\"\\"Reuse presentation/interaction assets without executing the old note writer.\\"\\"\\"\\r\\n    tree = ast.parse((HERE / \\"build-preview.py\\").read_text(encoding=\\"utf-8-sig\\"))\\r\\n    result = {}\\r\\n    for node in tree.body:\\r\\n        if isinstance(node, ast.Assign):\\r\\n            for target in node.targets:\\r\\n                if isinstance(target, ast.Name) and target.id in {\\"css\\", \\"js\\", \\"model\\"}:\\r\\n                    result[target.id] = ast.literal_eval(node.value)\\r\\n    if set(result) != {\\"css\\", \\"js\\", \\"model\\"}:\\r\\n        raise ValueError(\\"Original preview presentation assets could not be located\\")\\r\\n    return result\\r\\n\\r\\n\\r\\ndef student_source(text):\\r\\n    text = re.sub(r\\"\\\\A---\\\\s*\\\\n.*?\\\\n---\\\\s*\\\\n\\", \\"\\", text, count=1, flags=re.S)\\r\\n    divider = re.search(r\\"\\\\n---\\\\s*\\\\n\\\\s*## Notes for me\\", text)\\r\\n    if not divider:\\r\\n        raise ValueError(\\"Private recap divider missing; refusing to include private notes\\")\\r\\n    return text[:divider.start()].strip()\\r\\n\\r\\n\\r\\ndef inline(text):\\r\\n    value = html.escape(text)\\r\\n    value = re.sub(r\\"\\\\*\\\\*([^*]+)\\\\*\\\\*\\", r\\"<strong>\\\\1</strong>\\", value)\\r\\n    value = re.sub(r\\"`([^`]+)`\\", r\\"<code>\\\\1</code>\\", value)\\r\\n    return value\\r\\n\\r\\n\\r\\nclass Renderer:\\r\\n    def __init__(self, model):\\r\\n        self.counter = 0\\r\\n        self.model = model\\r\\n        self.model_used = False\\r\\n\\r\\n    def check(self, prompt, answer):\\r\\n        self.counter += 1\\r\\n        index = self.counter\\r\\n        return f\'\'\'<section class=\\"check\\" data-check=\\"{index}\\"><form><label for=\\"answer-{index}\\">{inline(prompt)}</label><textarea id=\\"answer-{index}\\" rows=\\"3\\" required placeholder=\\"Write your answer and explain why.\\"></textarea><button type=\\"submit\\">Check my answer</button></form><div class=\\"feedback\\" hidden tabindex=\\"-1\\"><strong>Compare your reasoning</strong><p>{inline(answer)}</p><p class=\\"small\\">Your response stays above. Compare the direction, the complete conditions, and the strength of the claim. This exercise does not automatically grade your reasoning.</p><button class=\\"again\\" type=\\"button\\">Try again</button></div><noscript><p><strong>Worked answer:</strong> {inline(answer)}</p></noscript></section>\'\'\'\\r\\n\\r\\n    def prose(self, block):\\r\\n        lines = block.splitlines()\\r\\n        if all(re.match(r\\"^\\\\d+\\\\. \\", line) for line in lines):\\r\\n            return \\"<ol>\\"+\\"\\".join(\\"<li>\\"+inline(re.sub(r\\"^\\\\d+\\\\. \\", \\"\\", line))+\\"</li>\\" for line in lines)+\\"</ol>\\"\\r\\n        if all(line.startswith(\\"- \\") for line in lines):\\r\\n            return \\"<ul>\\"+\\"\\".join(\\"<li>\\"+inline(line[2:])+\\"</li>\\" for line in lines)+\\"</ul>\\"\\r\\n        if block.startswith(\\"|\\"):\\r\\n            if not self.model_used and \\"Should undergo mediation\\" in block:\\r\\n                self.model_used = True\\r\\n                return self.model\\r\\n            rows = [line.strip().strip(\\"|\\").split(\\"|\\") for line in lines if line.strip().startswith(\\"|\\")]\\r\\n            rows = [[cell.strip() for cell in row] for row in rows]\\r\\n            rows = [row for row in rows if not all(re.match(r\\"^:?-+:?$\\", cell) for cell in row)]\\r\\n            if not rows:\\r\\n                raise ValueError(\\"Empty Markdown table\\")\\r\\n            return \'<div class=\\"table-scroll\\"><table class=\\"decision-table\\"><thead><tr>\'+\\"\\".join(\\"<th scope=\\\\\\"col\\\\\\">\\"+inline(cell)+\\"</th>\\" for cell in rows[0])+\\"</tr></thead><tbody>\\"+\\"\\".join(\\"<tr>\\"+\\"\\".join(\\"<td>\\"+inline(cell)+\\"</td>\\" for cell in row)+\\"</tr>\\" for row in rows[1:])+\\"</tbody></table></div>\\"\\r\\n        if (\\" → \\" in block or \\" -> \\" in block) and len(block) < 150:\\r\\n            return \'<p class=\\"formula\\">\'+inline(block).replace(\\"\\\\n\\", \\"<br>\\")+\\"</p>\\"\\r\\n        return \\"<p>\\"+inline(block).replace(\\"\\\\n\\", \\" \\")+\\"</p>\\"\\r\\n\\r\\n    def render(self, text, prefix):\\r\\n        blocks = re.split(r\\"\\\\n\\\\s*\\\\n\\", text.strip())\\r\\n        rendered = []\\r\\n        chapter = False\\r\\n        index = 0\\r\\n        chapter_count = 0\\r\\n        while index < len(blocks):\\r\\n            block = blocks[index].strip()\\r\\n            if block.startswith(\\"# \\"):\\r\\n                index += 1\\r\\n                continue\\r\\n            if block.startswith(\\"## \\"):\\r\\n                if chapter:\\r\\n                    rendered.append(\\"</section>\\")\\r\\n                chapter_count += 1\\r\\n                rendered.append(f\'<section class=\\"chapter\\" id=\\"{prefix}-{chapter_count}\\"><h2>\'+inline(block[3:])+\\"</h2>\\")\\r\\n                chapter = True\\r\\n            elif block.startswith(\\"### \\"):\\r\\n                rendered.append(\\"<h3>\\"+inline(block[4:])+\\"</h3>\\")\\r\\n            elif index+1 < len(blocks) and blocks[index+1].startswith(\\"**Worked answer:**\\"):\\r\\n                answer = blocks[index+1][len(\\"**Worked answer:**\\"):].strip()\\r\\n"}'}]

text(await tools.apply_patch("*** Begin Patch\n*** Add File: C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os/brainstorms/2026-09-30-yahunatan-post-session-recap/concept-lesson-v3.md\n+# Facts, Rules, and What Follows\n+\n+A rule tells you what follows **if a condition is met**. A fact tells you whether you have reached that condition. Put them together, and you can prove something new.\n+\n+Suppose a program has this policy: **Every application submitted after the deadline requires special approval.** One application was submitted after the deadline. That application requires special approval. We have a rule, a fact that fits its starting condition, and a conclusion the rule guarantees.\n+\n+Change the fact, and the conclusion can change. An application that requires special approval need not have been late. It could require approval for another reason. The policy gives one route to that requirement, not the only route.\n+\n+<!-- FACT-LAB -->\n+\n+## 01 | Start with the fact you actually have\n+\n+The rule applies to every late application. The fact identifies one of those applications. We can now apply the rule to that particular case.\n+\n+**Rule:** Every application submitted after the deadline requires special approval.\n+\n+**Fact:** This application was submitted after the deadline.\n+\n+**What follows:** This application requires special approval.\n+\n+The rule alone does not tell us that any applications were submitted late. We needed the fact to establish that we had a case to which the rule applies. A conditional rule can be true even if nothing meets its starting condition.\n+\n+Now give the two complete ideas short names. Let **L** mean “submitted after the deadline” and **R** mean “requires special approval.” The notation saves space; it should not change the meaning.\n+\n+`L -> R` means: if L is true, R must be true.\n+\n+**Try it:** Every file marked confidential requires restricted storage. This file is marked confidential. What follows? Does the rule alone establish that a confidential file exists?\n+\n+**Worked answer:** This file requires restricted storage. The fact establishes that this particular file is confidential. The rule alone establishes a requirement for any confidential file, without establishing that any such file exists.\n+\n+## 02 | Use the rule forward, or use its contrapositive\n+\n+There are two ways to use the application policy. A late application must require approval. An application that does **not** require approval cannot have been late. If it had been late, the policy would require approval, contradicting the fact we were given.\n+\n+<!-- TWO-MOVES -->\n+\n+**Forward:** Submitted late -> Requires approval.\n+\n+**Contrapositive:** Does not require approval -> Was not submitted late.\n+\n+The contrapositive states the same rule from its other end. Reverse the direction **and negate both complete ideas**. Simply reversing the arrow would say that requiring approval proves lateness. The original policy never said that.\n+\n+Simply negating both ideas without reversing would also add something: “not late -> does not require approval.” An on-time application could require approval for a different reason. That situation does not break the policy.\n+\n+The only combination the original rule rules out is **late, but does not require approval**. The other three combinations can fit the rule.\n+\n+<!-- CASE-GRID -->\n+\n+**Try it:** Every confidential file requires restricted storage. This file does not require restricted storage. What follows? What if all you know is that it does require restricted storage?\n+\n+**Worked answer:** If it does not require restricted storage, it is not confidential. That is the contrapositive. If it does require restricted storage, its confidentiality remains unknown. A nonconfidential file could require restricted storage for another reason.\n+\n+## 03 | Link rules when the whole middle idea matches\n+\n+Add a second policy: **Every application that requires special approval needs a supervisor's signature.** The first rule gets us to “requires special approval.” The second starts at that exact idea. We can carry the application through both rules.\n+\n+**First rule:** Submitted late -> Requires special approval.\n+\n+**Second rule:** Requires special approval -> Needs a supervisor's signature.\n+\n+**Combined rule:** Submitted late -> Needs a supervisor's signature.\n+\n+In letters, the pattern is `L -> R` and `R -> S`, so `L -> S`. If a particular application was late, both requirements follow for that application. Without a fact establishing a late application, we still have a combined rule rather than proof that a late application exists.\n+\n+You can also work backward through the combined rule. If an application does **not** need a supervisor's signature, it cannot require special approval under the second policy. If it does not require special approval, it cannot have been late under the first policy.\n+\n+**Backward chain:** Does not need signature -> Does not require approval -> Was not late.\n+\n+<!-- CHAIN-LAB -->\n+\n+### Matching a word is not enough\n+\n+“Requires special approval” and “has received special approval” both mention approval. They describe different conditions. A requirement can exist before anyone fulfills it. You cannot link a rule ending in the first phrase to a rule starting in the second unless another statement connects them.\n+\n+This is why you should diagram **complete ideas**, not isolated words. Keep words such as “requires,” “has,” “should,” and “may” inside the condition they qualify.\n+\n+**Try it:** Every late application requires special approval. Every application that has received special approval has a supervisor's signature. Does a late application necessarily have a supervisor's signature? Explain the missing connection.\n+\n+**Worked answer:** No. A late application requires approval, but it may not have received it. The second rule applies to applications that have received approval. Nothing says the late application's requirement has been fulfilled.\n+\n+## 04 | A shared starting point does not create a chain\n+\n+Compare a different pair of rules: **Every late application requires special approval. Every late application needs a supervisor's signature.** Both rules start at “late.” Neither starts where the other ends.\n+\n+<!-- SHARED-TRIGGER -->\n+\n+If a fact tells us an application was late, we can apply **both** rules to it. That application requires approval and needs a signature. But we cannot conclude that every application requiring approval needs a signature. An on-time application might require approval for another reason without needing a signature.\n+\n+Likewise, two rules that end at the same idea do not connect their starting conditions. If confidential files require restricted storage and medical files require restricted storage, a confidential file need not be a medical file. Restricted storage could cover both types separately.\n+\n+Before joining two arrows, find the destination of the first and the starting condition of the second. They must match, including any negation or qualifier. If they do not match, keep the rules separate or see whether a valid contrapositive produces a match.\n+\n+**Try it:** Every member of a review panel has training. Every member of that panel has a security badge. Must everyone with training have a security badge? What follows if Dana is a panel member?\n+\n+**Worked answer:** Training alone does not guarantee a badge. A trained person could be outside the panel. Because Dana is a panel member, both rules apply to Dana: Dana has training and a security badge.\n+\n+## 05 | Keep the kind of claim intact\n+\n+A rule can tell us what someone **should** do or what they **may** do. That is different from telling us what they actually did.\n+\n+Suppose **every damaged item should be inspected**. If this item is damaged, it should be inspected. We cannot conclude it has been inspected. If an item should not be inspected, then it is not damaged under that rule. But “has not been inspected” is not the same as “should not be inspected.” An inspection could be overdue.\n+\n+Suppose **a visitor may enter the archive only if the visitor has a badge**. A badge is necessary for permission to enter. The rule is **May enter -> Has a badge**. Without a badge, the visitor does not have permission to enter under this rule. Having a badge alone does not establish permission; another requirement might still apply.\n+\n+<!-- MODALITY -->\n+\n+The rule concerns permission. It does not prove that someone physically cannot enter without permission. Keep the same kind of claim throughout your reasoning: a requirement stays a requirement, advice stays advice, and permission stays permission.\n+\n+**Try it:** Every damaged item should be inspected. This damaged item has not been inspected. Does that violate the rule? What can you conclude about what should happen?\n+\n+**Worked answer:** The item should be inspected. Its not having been inspected does not contradict the claim about what should happen. The rule does not state that every required inspection actually occurs.\n+\n+## 06 | Negate the whole condition\n+\n+Some conditions contain more than one part. Suppose **a document may be released only if it has been reviewed AND authorized**. Permission requires both parts. If either part is missing, the combined requirement is not satisfied.\n+\n+**Rule:** May be released -> Reviewed AND authorized.\n+\n+**Contrapositive:** Not reviewed OR not authorized -> May not be released.\n+\n+To negate “reviewed AND authorized,” you only need at least one failure. You do not need both parts to fail. “Not both” does not mean “neither.”\n+\n+Now suppose **a visitor may enter only if the visitor has a badge OR an invitation**. This requirement allows either route, including both. To fail the entire requirement, the visitor must have neither.\n+\n+**Rule:** May enter -> Has badge OR has invitation.\n+\n+**Contrapositive:** No badge AND no invitation -> May not enter.\n+\n+<!-- NEGATION -->\n+\n+Negating a compound condition changes **AND to OR**, or **OR to AND**, while negating each part. For the original AND requirement, one missing part is enough. For the original OR requirement, both routes must be missing.\n+\n+**Try it:** A document may be released only if it has been reviewed and authorized. It has been reviewed but not authorized. What follows? Does the contrapositive require us to conclude it was not reviewed?\n+\n+**Worked answer:** The document may not be released. Missing authorization is enough to fail the combined requirement. We do not conclude that review is missing too; the fact says review occurred.\n+\n+## 07 | Decide what the information lets you do\n+\n+Start by separating **facts about a case** from **rules about cases**. Then choose the move the information supports.\n+\n+| What you have | The move | What to check |\n+| --- | --- | --- |\n+| A fact meeting a rule's starting condition | Apply the rule forward | Does the whole fact match the whole condition? |\n+| A fact denying a rule's required result | Use the contrapositive | Did you reverse and negate both complete ideas? |\n+| Two rules with a matching middle condition | Link the rules | Are direction, negation, and qualifiers identical? |\n+| Only the required result, or only the absence of the starting condition | Leave the other side open | Could another explanation fit the rule? |\n+\n+When an inference seems tempting, try to build a case in which the statements are true and the proposed conclusion is false. If you can, the conclusion is not guaranteed. The counterexamples above work this way: an on-time application can still require approval; a requirement can remain unfulfilled; a trained person can be outside the panel.\n+\n+## 08 | Mix the moves\n+\n+For each problem, write what follows and name the move. If a proposed conclusion is not guaranteed, explain a situation that would make it false while keeping the given statements true.\n+\n+**Practice 1:** Every confidential file requires restricted storage. File K does not require restricted storage. What follows?\n+\n+**Worked answer:** File K is not confidential. Use the contrapositive: does not require restricted storage -> not confidential.\n+\n+**Practice 2:** Every late application requires special approval. Every application requiring special approval needs a supervisor's signature. Application J was late. What follows?\n+\n+**Worked answer:** Application J requires special approval and needs a supervisor's signature. Apply the first rule to the fact, then carry its result through the second rule.\n+\n+**Practice 3:** Every panel member has training. Every panel member has a security badge. Lee has training. Must Lee have a badge?\n+\n+**Worked answer:** No. Lee could be a trained person outside the panel who has no badge. The two rules share a starting condition. Training alone does not establish that Lee meets it.\n+\n+**Practice 4:** A document may be released only if it has been reviewed and authorized. Document M has not been reviewed. What follows? Must it also lack authorization?\n+\n+**Worked answer:** Document M may not be released. One missing requirement is enough. Whether it has authorization remains open; it could have authorization but lack review.\n+\n+## 09 | Take this into your assigned work\n+\n+Do the **conditional-diagramming drills first**, then move to the **LSAT questions**. In the drills, write the complete conditions before shortening them. Check your arrow and its contrapositive. When two rules appear, look for an exact match before combining them.\n+\n+In the LSAT questions, separate the facts from the rules before choosing an answer. Your job is to identify what the statements guarantee. If an answer reverses the rule, drops a qualifier, or turns a requirement into an accomplished action, test it with a counterexample.\n+\n+Keep this question nearby: **What do I actually have, and which move does it support?**\n*** End Patch"));
